# iHYPE Design Sync Workflow
# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# How each design iteration flows from Claude Design → ihype.org
#
# PIPELINE:
#   Claude Design (.dc.html) → GitHub (src/app/*.tsx) → Cloudflare → ihype.org
#
# AGENTS:
#   Claude Design  — owns .dc.html files + design system
#   Claude Code    — owns src/app/ + API + database
#   Both           — read this file to stay in sync

---

## Step 1 · Design changes in Claude Design

When a page is updated, this file is edited:
  - DESIGN_SYNC.md → add entry to [PENDING CHANGES] below
  - .dc.html page updated
  - Ready for Claude Code to implement

---

## Step 2 · Claude Code reads DESIGN_SYNC.md

Claude Code checks [PENDING CHANGES] and for each entry:
  1. Opens the .dc.html URL listed
  2. Translates HTML/CSS/JS → Next.js page + components
  3. Wires the API calls listed under each change
  4. Marks the entry as [DONE] with commit SHA

---

## Step 3 · GitHub → Cloudflare → ihype.org (automatic)

  git push origin main
  → GitHub Actions: deploy-production.yml runs
  → OpenNext builds → Cloudflare Workers deploys
  → Live at ihype.org within ~2 minutes

---

### ⚠ Route-ownership resolutions
**Updated 2026-06-30 (v2) — supersedes earlier legal-split decision:**
- **Legal.dc.html is the single CANONICAL legal page** with three in-page tabs (Privacy · Terms · Charter) serving `/legal`, `/privacy`, and `/terms`. Standalone `Privacy.dc.html` and `Terms.dc.html` were **DELETED** (redundant) — their content is merged into Legal's tabs. Charter also keeps its standalone `/charter` page (Charter.dc.html). Route `/privacy` and `/terms` to Legal (deep-link the matching tab).
- **`Radio.dc.html` (DJ Studio) DELETED** — superseded by WebRadio.dc.html, which now owns `/radio`. DJ go-live / schedule / manage / edit actions repointed to **Studio.dc.html** (creator workbench). If a dedicated DJ broadcast console is needed later, add it as a Studio tab, not a separate page.
- **`Discover.dc.html` DELETED** — redundant with Search + Charts + Seeds (FanHome). Its links were removed from Sitemap.
- **404 vs Offline** — unchanged: 404.dc.html → `not-found.tsx`; Offline.dc.html → `error.tsx`/`global-error.tsx` + `/offline`.
- **Global footer** — NavShell now injects a fixed footer bar (Legal | Support | © 2026 ihype.org) on every page; header + footer are solid (no transparency/blur).

## Design Token Map · DS → Next.js globals.css

Claude Code: copy these into src/app/globals.css

```css
/* iHYPE Design Tokens — auto-synced from DS 39bcce7b */

:root {
  /* Backgrounds */
  --bg:  #0a0805;
  --bg2: #100d09;
  --bg3: #1a1611;
  --bg4: #241d18;

  /* Text */
  --ink:  #f0ebe5;
  --line: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.06);

  /* Brand */
  --accent:    #ff5029;   /* iHYPE orange-red */

  /* Roles */
  --fan:       #b983ff;   /* purple */
  --venue:     #22e5d4;   /* teal */
  --promoter:  #ff3e9a;   /* pink */
  --artist:    #ff5029;   /* same as accent */

  /* Typography */
  --font-display: 'Syne', sans-serif;         /* 800 weight only */
  --font-body:    'DM Sans', sans-serif;       /* 400/500 */
  --font-mono:    'JetBrains Mono', monospace; /* labels, ALL CAPS */
  --font-serif:   'Instrument Serif', serif;   /* italic, editorial */

  /* Radius */
  --radius-sm:  8px;
  --radius-md:  10px;
  --radius-lg:  24px;
  --radius-xl:  32px;
  --radius-pill: 9999px;

  /* Motion */
  --ease-spring: cubic-bezier(0.2, 0.7, 0.3, 1);
  --duration-fast: 150ms;
  --duration-base: 200ms;
  --duration-slow: 300ms;
}
```

## API Client · lib/api.js → src/lib/api.ts

Claude Code: the design API client (lib/api.js) maps 1:1 to the Next.js API routes.
For server components, use `db.*` directly. For client components, use fetch or SWR.

```typescript
// Pattern used in all .dc.html pages:
const shows = await API.events.list({ city: 'San Francisco' });

// Next.js server component equivalent:
const shows = await db.event.findMany({
  where: { city: 'San Francisco', status: 'PUBLISHED' },
  orderBy: { startsAt: 'asc' }
});
```

---

Endpoints marked **GAP** do not exist in `lib/api.js` yet; Claude Code must add them to **both** `lib/api.js` (design) and `src/app/api/*` (backend) so the two sides line up when merged. Until a GAP is filled, the page runs on `MOCK` fallback.

### Consolidated GAP list — add to lib/api.js AND src/app/api (both agents)
1. `GET /fans/:slug` → `API.fans.get(slug)` — public fan profile
2. `GET /me/notifications` + `POST /notifications/:id/read` → `API.notifications.list()` / `.markRead(id)`
3. `GET /payout/:eventId` → `API.payouts.get(eventId)` — post-event receipt (45/45/10)
4. `POST /verify` → `API.verify.submit(data)` — applicant proof submission (distinct from admin review)
5. `POST /beta/join` → `API.beta.join(email)` — TestFlight waitlist
6. `GET /radio-shows/:id/replay` → `API.radio.replay(id)` + live/record: `POST /radio/live`, `POST /radio/record`
7. `GET /artists/:slug/radio-archive` → `API.artists.radioArchive(slug)` — DJ show archive

> **Merge rule:** a GAP endpoint must be added to `lib/api.js` in the SAME change that implements the page's `.tsx`, and the route must exist under `src/app/api/`. Add each new method to the "API Client" 1:1 map above so the design ↔ backend surfaces never drift again.

---

1. **Design is source of truth for:** layout, copy, colors, component structure
2. **Code is source of truth for:** data shapes, auth logic, business rules
3. **Never diverge on:** 45/45/10 split, $0 fees copy, role colors
4. **Always sync on:** major layout changes, new pages, removed features

## PENDING CHANGES
<!-- Claude Design adds entries here. Claude Code marks them [DONE]. -->

| # | Page | What Changed | API Calls | Status |
|---|------|-------------|-----------|--------|
| 323 | `src/lib/media-backfill.ts` (new), `src/app/api/cron/route.ts` (`media-backfill` job) | **The catch-up for everything written while object storage was off (2026-08-31).** Row 322 fixed the mechanism; it did nothing for the rows already carrying their bytes. Every profile image, cover art, ad spot and uploaded track from before that fix is base64 inside Postgres, and `ArtistMediaAsset.fileDataBase64` holds whole audio files. **It is a cron job and not a script, for a reason that is easy to get wrong**: the R2 binding is handed to the Worker by Cloudflare and does not exist in a Node process, so nothing outside a request can write to the bucket without reintroducing the credentials row 322 removed. `GET /api/cron?job=media-backfill` measures; `&apply=1&limit=N` moves. Three properties make it safe against production: it is a **dry run unless `apply` is set**; it is **idempotent**, because only a value still starting with `data:` is a candidate, so a migrated row is invisible to the next pass; and it **never drops the inline copy until the R2 write returns `storageType: 'r2'`**, so a failed upload leaves the row exactly as it was. Denormalized copies **cascade rather than re-upload** — `FanPlaylistItem` and `FanFavoriteMedia` carry their own `url`/`artworkUrl` snapshots, and they are repointed by `mediaId` at the asset's new URL, so one object is stored, not three. **`Profile.verificationProofUrl` is excluded, deliberately and permanently**: those are identity and ownership proofs, `/api/verify` keeps them inline on purpose, and moving them would silently undo that decision — the `/cdn` allowlist refuses the prefix, but an edge rule for `/cdn/*` may exist in the dashboard where this project's routes live, so the guarantee cannot rest on the allowlist alone. Proven end to end against a scratch database seeded to look like the pre-fix state: 13 candidates / 15.9 MB measured with the database unchanged, then migrated over two passes (10 then 3, `more` flipping to false), 2 denormalized rows cascaded, **zero inline media left in any migrated column and the three verification proofs still inline**, a re-run reporting `migrated: 0`, and the migrated objects served back through `/cdn` byte-for-byte (4,725,820-byte m4a as `audio/mp4`, the PNG as `image/png`) while `verification/` and a `../` traversal both answer 404. **Not on a schedule in `wrangler.cron.toml`**, on purpose: this is a one-off catch-up, and a recurring job whose steady state is "nothing to do" is one nobody reads the output of. | none | ✅ branch `claude/stripe-rehearsal-results` |
| 322 | `src/lib/object-storage.ts` (rewritten), `src/app/cdn/[...key]/route.ts` (new), `src/lib/runtime-env.ts`, `src/lib/audio-duration.ts`, `src/app/api/verify/route.ts` | **Object storage runs on the R2 BINDING now, and row 321's account of why it was broken was wrong in a way worth recording (2026-08-31).** Row 321 reasoned that the four `R2_*` credentials "can only be Worker secrets" and that `process.env` therefore could not see them. `wrangler secret list` against the live Worker settles it: **there are 22 secrets and not one is an R2 credential.** They were never set anywhere — not as secrets, not as `[vars]`. So the S3/SigV4 path in `object-storage.ts` had been dead for its entire life, and switching it to `readRuntimeEnv()` (row 321) could not by itself have turned anything on. **The fix is to stop needing credentials at all.** `wrangler.toml` has declared `[[r2_buckets]] binding = "R2"` all along, and the sibling `src/lib/r2.ts` has been using it successfully for track AUDIO the whole time — which is exactly why this survived: the biggest files went through the live implementation while everything else silently fell back. `object-storage.ts` is now ~200 lines of SigV4 replaced by `bucket.put`/`bucket.delete` through the binding, reached via a new `readRuntimeBinding()` on the same tested reader seam as `readRuntimeEnv` (so a fake bucket can be injected without a Worker, and `isObjectStorageConfigured()` stays SYNCHRONOUS — no call site changed). Measured after: a profile image comes back as `…/cdn/profile/<id>/…` instead of a base64 `data:` URL, and ad-audio upload stops answering 503. **`/cdn/[...key]` is new and closes a gap nobody had noticed**: `r2.ts` has emitted `/cdn/<key>` URLs since it was written and NOTHING in this repository ever served that path — either an edge rule maps it to the bucket (dashboard-managed, invisible here) or those URLs 404. The route removes the guess. **It serves PUBLIC PREFIXES ONLY** (`profile/`, `artist-media/`, `ads/`): a key is a bearer token, so the namespace list is a decision about who can read what. **Verification documents are deliberately kept OUT of R2 entirely** — they are identity and ownership proofs, and the allowlist alone is not enough protection when an edge rule for `/cdn/*` may exist outside the codebase; `/api/verify` now always stores inline, with the reasoning in place. Upgrading that later means R2 plus an admin-authenticated read route, never a public prefix. **Third defect, found by the walk on the way through**: `parseAudioDuration` read a WAV's `byteRate` at fmt+12, which is `sampleRate` — so every 16-bit file reported bytes-per-sample × channels times its true length. A real 20s mono file measured 40s; ordinary 16-bit stereo reads 4× long, meaning a 15-second spot was refused for exceeding 30 seconds and WAV ad uploads were effectively impossible. It also stored wrong `durationSecs` for uploaded WAV tracks, which is what the station counts minutes of music with for ad-break cadence. Both this and the binding change are pinned by tests that fail against the old code. | none | ✅ branch `claude/stripe-rehearsal-results` |
| 321 | `src/app/api/tickets/[serializedId]/qr/route.ts`, `src/lib/object-storage.ts`, `src/lib/usernames.ts`, `src/app/api/register/route.ts`, `CLAUDE.md` | **The three findings row 320 left open, closed (2026-08-31).** **(1) The ticket QR led nowhere.** It encoded `/api/tickets/:id/scan`, which is POST-only, so a phone camera opening it got **405** and the code printed on every ticket was decorative. It now encodes `buildTicketVerificationUrl()` — the same helper the EMAILED ticket QR already used, so the codebase's two ticket QRs can no longer disagree, and only this one was ever wrong. That URL redirects to the ticket page, which already draws the working "Mark as scanned" button for venue staff (`TicketVerificationCard`, gated on `canScan`) and shows holder/show/status to anyone else — so **no in-app scanner and no third-party app is needed**; the fix was one line pointing at a flow that already existed. It also drops a hardcoded `https://ihype.org`, which had every non-production QR pointing at the live site. Measured after: the QR's URL opens with GET (307), first scan accepted, replay refused 409. **(2) Object storage was reading `process.env`.** All four `R2_*` credentials, plus `R2_PUBLIC_URL`, now go through `readRuntimeEnv()`. None of them is a plain `[vars]` entry in `wrangler.toml`, so they can only be Worker secrets — and secrets never land on `process.env` under workerd. `isObjectStorageConfigured()` therefore answered FALSE in production however carefully the account was set up, and the failure is silent by construction: every caller has a fallback, so uploads kept "working" while base64-ing whole images and audio into Postgres rows, and `/api/advertise/audio-upload` — the one caller without a fallback — answered 503. Same shape as the bug that left email dead 36 days, which is why `runtime-env.ts` exists. Pinned by a test that fails against the old code: credentials present ONLY on the Cloudflare binding must configure storage. **(3) Signup refused ordinary names.** The handle is derived from the display name (the form never asks for one) and was then validated, so the derived value produced errors about a field the member had never seen. Measured, all refused: `Sarah O'Brien` (apostrophe), `Renée` (combining acute), `Bo` (the pattern's floor is 3), `Support` (reserved), and **every non-Latin name — `李明` normalises to an empty string, so they could not create an account at all** — plus the second `Sarah Smith`, refused 409 "An account with those credentials already exists". New pure `deriveUsernameCandidate()` sanitises (NFKD, drop combining marks, strip illegal characters, trim separators from both ends, cap 30) and returns null when nothing legal survives; the route then mints a random handle rather than refusing, de-duplicates a derived handle against a single prefix query, and applies the strict `isValidUsername` gate ONLY to a handle the member actually typed — a chosen one is theirs to fix, so its 400/409 is still correct. The unique constraint plus the existing P2002 branch remain the arbiter for a concurrent race. Measured after: `Sarah O'Brien -> sarahobrien`, `Renée Fleming -> reneefleming`, `李明 -> user…`, `Bo -> user…`, and two members sharing a display name both register. **Also corrected**: this file's row for `GET /api/radio/ad-clips` described a route deleted with `RadioShowCreator`; ads reach a listener through `getStationState()`/`GET /api/radio/station`, and the stale comment in `show-composer.ts` naming the old route is fixed. Verification: 904 unit tests, 21 e2e, and the alpha walk at **22 passed / 0 failed** (the two remaining BLOCKED are sandbox-only — Stripe rejects a `localhost` `business_url`, and this box holds no R2 credentials). | none | ✅ branch `claude/stripe-rehearsal-results` |
| 320 | `scripts/alpha-acceptance-walk.mts` (new), `src/lib/stripe.ts`, `src/app/api/profile/upload-graphic/route.ts`, `src/components/PageEditor.tsx` | **The alpha acceptance list, walked end to end against a real worker — and it found two production defects that every other check was structurally blind to (2026-08-31).** The 31-item list is now half Playwright (`creation-flows`/`engagement-flows`/`destructive-flows`/`ticket-transfer`/`auth`/`passkey`, 21 tests, all green) and half this script, which drives the API against a workerd build on a scratch Postgres using the REAL test assets — a 4.7 MB m4a and a 2000×1500 PNG, because both upload routes validate magic bytes and a synthesized buffer proves only that the validator can be fooled. **Defect 1 — saving a payment method was a hard 500 for every member.** `createPaymentMethodSetupSession` creates a `mode: 'setup'` Checkout Session with neither `currency` nor `payment_method_types`; setup mode offers dynamic payment methods and needs a currency to decide which, so Stripe answered `Missing required param: currency` and no card could ever be saved. Verified both ways against real test-mode Stripe with production URLs — the identical call minus the line fails, plus it returns a URL. Nothing caught it because the route is only reachable once paid ticketing is on, which it became the day before (row 319). **Defect 2 — profile images landed on the wrong page.** `POST /api/profile/upload-graphic` resolved the target with `findFirst({ where: { ownerId } })`: no `orderBy`, and the caller's `profileId` ignored outright — while `PageEditor` never sent one, though it already passes `profileId` on every other call it makes. Measured: an avatar uploaded FOR an artist page was written to that member's VENUE page, and the artist's stayed null. An artist who also runs a venue is the ordinary case, not a corner. Fixed on both sides, ownership re-checked server-side (`profileId` names a target, it never grants one), with a deterministic `orderBy` under the legacy no-id path; re-run confirms artist SET / venue untouched, the exact inverse of the failure. **Also confirmed working, with figures**: a real sale captures 1952c and writes three payables summing 1837c with the HYPE-link promoter earning 184c = 10.0%; cancellation refunds the unscanned order for real (`re_3UAW0n…`, 1865c) while SKIPPING the scanned one rather than clawing back an attended show; a replayed scan is refused 409. **Two items are environment-blocked, neither a code fault**: Connect onboarding cannot succeed on loopback (Stripe rejects a `localhost` `business_url` — the same create with `https://ihype.org/…` succeeds, proven by a two-arm probe in the script), and ad-audio upload needs four `R2_*` S3 credentials this sandbox lacks. **Three findings left open and reported, not fixed**: the ticket QR encodes a URL whose route is POST-only, so a phone camera opening it gets **405** and the QR is decorative (see the runbook note); `isObjectStorageConfigured()` reads `process.env` rather than `readRuntimeEnv`, the same shape that had email dead 36 days, and is worth verifying against production; and signup derives a username from the display NAME with no de-duplication, so the second "Sarah Smith" is refused with an error naming a credential she was never asked for (measured: 200 then 409). | none | ✅ branch `claude/stripe-rehearsal-results` |
| 319 | `wrangler.toml` (`FEATURE_ENABLE_TICKET_PAYMENTS`), `docs/runbooks/money-path-rehearsal.md` | **Paid ticketing is ON (2026-08-31, owner: "Do it!") — runbook step 3's first bullet, taken as the deliberate config change the flag's own comment demands.** The prerequisite that kept it false since 2026-08-27 is met in full: Connect signed up (Stripe-managed risk), rehearsal step 1 at 25/25 across all three settlement modes, step 2 walked against the real worker in all three modes — real transfers to real onboarded destinations, the payout cron run twice with `released: 0` on the second pass — the venue-direct refund defect fixed and re-proven, the dispute walk measured 8/8 (rows 315–318). Safety rails around the flip: the post-deploy smoke previously accepted "disabled-by-flag" as the approved state and now asserts FULL launch readiness — a missing live secret or a test key in production fails the deploy loudly; four green deploys today already prove `paymentsDisabledByFlag` was the sole blocker, i.e. the live `sk_live_` and `whsec_` are set. What stays visible-by-design: live mode has no connected accounts yet, so early sales settle PLATFORM with payables PENDING until payees onboard (the cron reports `skipped`, connect-health promotes on KYC). **The remaining act is the operator's**: the deliberate first sale — buy one cheap ticket with a real card, walk step 2's checks against production, cancel the show to refund — now written as a five-item checklist in the runbook's step-3 section. | 295 | `src/lib/mmm-play.ts` (new), `src/components/mmm/{MmmShell,MmmDock,MmmMusic,MmmTrackPlayIntent}.tsx`, `src/app/app/tracks/[hexId]/page.tsx`, `src/lib/__tests__/{mmm-play,mmm-transport-chain}.test.ts`, `e2e/mmm-shell.spec.ts` | **Universal play — the transport is never inert (2026-08-22, owner: "The music player needs universal play ability this is a music app after all").** Row 293 made the joystick able to start playback on Discover; everywhere else it was still a dead tap. A tap now resolves three things IN ORDER — pause the current track, else start what the surface registered, else **turn the radio on** — and it is the third branch that makes it universal rather than per-surface: MAP, ME, a profile and a ticket have nothing of their own to offer. `MmmShell.startRadio` fetches `/api/stations`, takes `defaultStationSlug`'s first entry (deterministic on purpose — the same tap in the same place must start the same thing) and plays it; failures are swallowed because the control's whole promise is "something will play" and there is no surface to render an error into. **Two surfaces turned out to be holding playable data and throwing it away.** `ChartsTab`'s own row type dropped `mediaUrl` and `artworkUrl`, which `/api/charts` has always returned — so the chart was the one MUSIC surface with playable rows that could not be played at all, a discarded field rather than a missing feature. And `/app/tracks/[hexId]` had **no play control of any kind** and its query did not even select `storageUrl`: a track's own page, in a music app, with no way to hear the track. `MmmTrackPlayIntent` is a null-rendering client registrar, which is a smaller thing to cross the server/client line with than making the page a client component for one hook. `RecommendedTab` had the same shape — rows carrying `mediaUrl` used only to build a link — and now plays the list. **`src/lib/mmm-play.ts` is the one conversion, and the filter is the point:** a row with no stored audio is dropped, because the player advances by INDEX and a dead entry is a stall rather than a skip. `RadioTab` had the only correct version of that inline; three other surfaces had none. Every DEMO row carries `mediaUrl: null`, which is now load-bearing rather than cosmetic — `toQueue` drops them, so the transport cannot play placeholder content on a surface that is showing it. The rows themselves stay LINKS on every list: turning them into play buttons is a design change nothing asked for, and the transport is the control this is about. Tested where it can be: `toQueue`/`defaultStationSlug` pure (9 cases, including the unplayable-row drop and the no-identity drop), the dock's three-way chain restated and asserted on ORDER and on never resolving to nothing, and an e2e parameterised over five surfaces — MAP and ME to prove the fallthrough, the music tabs to prove their own intents — which skips itself when the fixture has no playable audio anywhere rather than accepting either outcome. **Extended the same day to every remaining surface, and the Playlists caveat above was wrong**: `FanPlaylistItem` already stores a fully playable row — `url`, `title`, `artistName`, `artworkUrl`, `artistProfileSlug` — and `/api/fan-playlists` has always returned them. No endpoint change was needed; the tab's own `PlaylistRow` type kept a COUNT and discarded the items, which is the third instance of the same shape of bug in one file. Registered now: the Playlists tab (first playlist), `/app/playlists/[id]` (its items — the page selected four columns and not the audio), `/app/artists/[slug]` (published releases in the artist's own order, outside the tab condition so the joystick plays them from any section — the page listed releases and offered no way to hear one) and `/app/tracks/[hexId]`. `PlayableRow` now accepts `url` as well as `mediaUrl`, because those are one concept under two column names. `MmmTrackPlayIntent` is replaced by one `MmmPlayHere` for all four server pages. **And the naive version of that is a render loop, caught before shipping by simulating it rather than by CI:** `toQueue(rows)` returns a fresh array every render, so the callback identity churns, so the effect re-registers, so the provider's state changes, so every consumer re-renders — six renders, six state changes, measured; one with the queue memoised. Same fault that took the Workerd server down when this file was first added, arriving by a different route. The fix is `useRegisterQueue`, a hook that memoises on a DIGEST of the rows' identity and audio rather than on the array reference, so no call site can get it wrong; the key's properties are tested directly (stable for a fresh array with equal contents, changed by a new URL at the same identity, by reordering, and by a longer list with the same head). **The loop was NOT what CI was failing on, and diagnosing it from the failure signature was wrong twice.** Both runs died with ~20 tests all reporting `ECONNREFUSED :8787` — the same shape as the earlier `MmmPlayIntent` loop — so the memo fix looked like the answer and the next run failed identically. The actual cause is in the log and is unambiguous: `V8 fatal error … JavaScript heap out of memory` after the `wrangler dev` process heap reached **1393 MB**. The server died and everything after it cascaded; the FIRST failure carries a different error from the rest, which is the tell that was there to read. And the cause was this PR's own TEST code: the new parameterised joystick test walked every station and fetched each one's tracks inside `page.evaluate`, per surface and per retry — eight stations across seven surfaces with three attempts is ~168 station-track queries through one long-lived process. The previous PR's version of the same test used a DOM count and passed. Now two requests, cached for the whole file, against the FIRST station only — which is also a truer precondition, since `defaultStationSlug` is the station the fallback actually picks. A test that needs a database is expensive by nature; making its precondition cheap is the difference between expensive and fatal. | none | ✅ Done (code) — merged in #741 (a519ca4) |
| 294 | `src/app/mmm.css` (`.mmm-map-canvas`), `CLAUDE.md`, `design/handoff-console-2026-08-21/UPSTREAM_FIXES.md` | **The map is sepia, and the reason it could not be was an operator order (2026-08-22, owner: "Map needs to be sepia").** Row 292 measured the design's filter honestly — `sepia(.92) saturate(.52) contrast(1.06) brightness(1.04) hue-rotate(-8deg)` turns OSM water `#aad3df` into `#fffee4` and land `#f2efe9` into `#ffffff`, **1.02:1 at 0 degrees of hue separation**, the harbour and the land the same colour — and drew the wrong conclusion from them: that sepia is incompatible with a coastal map. It is not. **The fault is that `brightness` runs LAST.** The sepia matrix has row sums of 1.35 (red) and 1.20 (green), so a near-white basemap tile clips at 255 in both channels and emerges white with a faint yellow cast, and `brightness(1.04)` guarantees it. There is no tone left to see because the highlights were destroyed before the tone was applied. Darkening FIRST gives it somewhere to live: `brightness(.72) sepia(1) saturate(1.1) contrast(1.15)` measures water `#c8ae7f` against land `#ffe3a7` — **1.71:1, land hue 41 degrees at 35% saturation** — against 1.50:1 and 3% for the hue-preserving tone it replaces. So **full sepia separates land from water better than not-sepia did**, because the separation becomes tonal rather than chromatic, which is what a sepia print is rather than a compromise on the way to one. Verified three ways rather than argued: the sRGB arithmetic, a parameter search over sepia/brightness/contrast/saturate for the strongest sepia holding a visible coastline, and all three variants rendered in Chromium against voyager's real palette with this file's own grain and ruled grid composited on top. Legibility measured on the composited ground rather than assumed — pin ink 12.72:1 on land and 7.52:1 on water (was 15.07 and 10.04), both far clear of AA; `--accent` as a bare fill 2.70 → 2.28 on land, which is why it is a pin's stem and border and never its label. **The first search returned nothing, and that was the finding**: it applied `brightness` after `sepia`, exactly as the design does, so every candidate clipped and no combination could satisfy a saturated land. `UPSTREAM_FIXES.md` item 12 is rewritten from "do not adopt this tone" to "move `brightness` to the front and take it below 1" — the tone that bundle is reaching for is available, and the order is what withholds it. | none | ✅ Done (code) — merged in #740 (0f2904f) |
| 293 | `src/app/mmm.css`, `scripts/{audit-css,lint-source}.mjs`, `src/components/mmm/{MmmPlayIntent,MmmDock,MmmShell,MmmMusic,MmmSeedDeck,MmmFullPlayer}.tsx`, `src/components/AlwaysOnStation.tsx`, `e2e/mmm-shell.spec.ts` | **Three phone reports, and all three were a rule or a glyph that looked right in source (2026-08-22).** **(1) 273px of empty cream between MUSIC's search bar and its first card**, with the deck pushed below the fold — reported as "space between search and content too wide requires scroll". The registration is a function rather than a track, because "play" on Discover means the card on screen and a captured one goes stale a swipe later. `playCard` was lifted out of an inline arrow so the card and the joystick start the same thing rather than two copies of it. **(3) Four more blue emoji squares, this time in our OWN components.** The FE0E fix from row 292 was applied by `vendor:ds` and so covered only the generated components; `MmmSeedDeck`, `MmmFullPlayer`, `MmmMusic` and `AlwaysOnStation` each render a bare `\u25b6` play glyph, and every one of them draws as a blue rounded square on iOS — visible in the same screenshot as a blue button on a walnut-and-brass surface. | 292 | `scripts/{vendor-ds-components,measure-dock,measure-datepicker}.mjs`, `src/components/ds/{JoystickTransport,FullPlayer}.tsx` (regenerated), `src/components/mmm/MmmMap.tsx`, `src/lib/map-dates.ts`, `src/app/{mmm,mobile-fit}.css`, `e2e/mmm-shell.spec.ts`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `design/handoff-console-2026-08-21/UPSTREAM_FIXES.md` | **Five corrections from driving the shipped dock and map on a real iPhone (2026-08-22).** Every one was invisible to the whole check suite and to every desktop browser, which is the point worth keeping: this repo can measure geometry, colour, type and routes, and none of those saw any of it. **(1) The joystick rendered as three blue squares.** `\u25c0` and `\u25b6` carry `Emoji=Yes` — an emoji variant exists — even though their default is text presentation, and WebKit serves the colour glyph from Apple Color Emoji anyway; `\u25b2` and `\u25bc` have no emoji variant and came through as the intended engraved triangles, which is exactly what the screenshot showed. Fixed in `vendor:ds` as a documented transform (append **U+FE0E**, VARIATION SELECTOR-15 — the OPPOSITE of the FE0F that DS8's no-emoji rule and `audit:design` are about) rather than in the generated file, which the next run reverts, or in a wrapper, which cannot reach a string literal inside a component body. 4 occurrences, 2 components — `FullPlayer`'s play button had it too. **(2) The dial's neighbour hints came back, on the scale row.** They had been DELETED to stop them ellipsising the station name, and that was the wrong half of the trade — a tuner you cannot see past is a stepper. So the dock read as flat cream glass in a brass ring. Applied to the dock's dial only, since the two components share the class name and only this one sits on walnut. Note `--lamp-rgb` is NOT used: the design system defines it, this codebase does not, and `rgba(var(--lamp-rgb), .95)` would have dropped the declaration silently — worse than the literal it replaced. **(4) The map's vintage overlay is a RULED GRID, and the design's tone is measured and refused.** "Grainy but doesn't have the vintage overlay" was right on both counts: the wash, foxing and vignette were here, but `map.html`'s `.grain` also draws a hairline every 33px in BOTH directions, and that grid is the single cue that reads as an old survey chart. Its tone treatment is not: computed in sRGB as CSS filters are specified, `sepia(.92) saturate(.52) contrast(1.06) brightness(1.04) hue-rotate(-8deg)` turns OSM water `#aad3df` into `#fffee4` and land `#f2efe9` into `#ffffff` — **1.02:1, 0 degrees of hue separation**, the harbour and the land the same colour, on the design's own basemap. The shipped hue-preserving tone keeps 1.50:1 and 147 degrees. Second time this conclusion has been reached here (the first was by rendering five variants), so it is now numbers in the CSS. **(5) The map's chrome is one search bar.** "Near me" retired — the map calls `getCurrentPosition` on arrival, which reverses MOBILE.md's never-on-launch rule for this one capability at the owner's direction, while deliberately NOT reinstating the primer SHEET whose scrim covered the whole shell and broke the nav. | 291 | `next.config.mjs`, `src/lib/rate-limit.ts`, `scripts/smoke-production.mjs`, `src/lib/__tests__/{next-config-redirects,rate-limit-timeout}.test.ts` | **The two production faults the dock work surfaced, both invisible from inside the repo (2026-08-22).** **(1) Bare `/legal` returned a hard HTTP 500** — a URL that ships in signup consent copy, the cookie banner, sent email and the app-store listings, so it is among the least likely to be clicked by anyone working on the app, and `/legal?tab=privacy` answered a clean 307 the whole time. The rule was `has: [{ type: 'query', key: 'tab', value: '(?<tab>.*)' }]` → `/info?tab=:tab`. **`.*` matches the empty string**, and OpenNext's `has` matcher tests `new RegExp(value).test(query[key] ?? '')` — so a request carrying no `tab` at all MATCHED, captured `tab: ''`, and handed that to path-to-regexp's `compile`, which throws on an empty required param rather than emitting an empty one. The throw is the 500. **Next's own router rejects an absent value before it runs the regex**, so this could not reproduce under `next start`, and it **never reached Sentry** either: it is thrown in the routing layer, ahead of the Next handler and our instrumentation. Three layers of local verification and a monitored production, and the only thing that could see it was curling the URL. The fix is one character; the guard is the general rule — **any `has` regex whose destination re-emits the capture must refuse the empty string** — asserted for every entry in `redirects()`, along with the `/legal` pair's ORDER, since OpenNext takes the first rule whose regex and `has` both match. The five legal aliases are now in `smoke-production.mjs`, which follows redirects and was verified against the live 500 before the fix went in. **(2) The rate limiter's 750ms default, and the 2026-07-28 fix that stopped one bucket short.** That fix found cold starts on the sign-in path and put `timeoutMs: 2500` on the five auth buckets, leaving 52 on 750ms. But the cold-start argument was never about auth — it is about bucket SHAPE, and a bucket keyed per user on an endpoint someone hits a few times a week has an object that is evicted between uses whatever the endpoint does. Sentry then showed `POST /api/discover/seeds/[id]/save` timing out **158 times and counting**, each one dropping that member to the KV fallback at **half** their configured limit: a refusal caused by the limiter, not by their traffic. Default now **1800ms**, with **KV cut 1500 → 1000ms** so the worst case (both deadlines expire) is 2.8s — under the 3s the module's own comment has always refused, which is the constraint that stops this becoming a blanket 2500. The pair is a combined budget and the arithmetic is written above the constant; the test mirrors the default and says why. | none | ✅ Done (code) — merged in #738 (61d0b7c) |

Now covered against a fake transaction client, including the two that cost money if they loosen: the payable filter is **PENDING-only**, so a refund cannot claw back a transfer that has already left the platform balance, and a lost race stops before voiding a ticket rather than decrementing capacity twice. `finalizeCapturedTicketOrder` too — one ticket per seat, payables summing to exactly the captured order, idempotent when Stripe reports a capture twice, and the two race outcomes distinguished. The half that needs a `sk_test_` key and a scratch database is written down as a runbook rather than left as folklore: buy, buy through a HYPE link, refund, scan, end the show, run the payout cron, **run it again and see `released: 0`** — a cron that pays twice is invisible until it happens. **(2) The console screens, audited rather than repainted.** Four of the five templates describe screens this app already renders correctly, because they were generated FROM its sources (settings carries all eight `MmmSettings` sections; info all six `mmm-me-panels` rows and two more; the profile tab sets are supersets). Stripe's line is labelled as the ORDER's, because the flat 30c is per transaction and dividing it by quantity would invent a figure Stripe never charged. **(3) Four checks moved from "run by hand" to CI.** `audit:routes --max-legacy=0` (the legacy shell has no rendered pages left, and zero is the number worth defending), `audit:retro` in the fast lane, `measure:dock` beside the other Chromium stages — and `src/components/ds/` was **missing from the depth step's pattern**, so a re-vendored knob could change the dock's geometry without selecting the stage that measures it. `audit:mobile` runs post-deploy against production, where its budgets are actually measured, and deliberately does not gate: `--strict` there would make a browser flake look like a broken deploy. **(4) `audit:retro` was losing every `design-exempt` marker to its own comment stripper** — block comments collapsed to a single space, so the line numbering it decided exemptions against no longer matched the source, and a marker written above the line it excuses was deleted before it could be read. The generator refuses anything whose shape it cannot verify (a SHA-1 pasted from the same Play Console screen is rejected by length) and `check:app-links` reads back what is on disk. `docs/app-store-review.md` names the four credential-blocked items in order of what they break, push first — it is half the defence against Apple's 4.2 rejection of a WebView pointed at a website. **`UPSTREAM_FIXES.md` is the batch going back to Claude Design**: eight measured defects, each of which deletes code here when it lands (the knob cap and dial shoulder scales that force two `!important` overrides, the four role hues failing AA in `tokens/colors.css`, 42 hardcoded colour literals, the hardcoded font families that resolve to nothing in this app, `TicketQR` needing its warning in its name, ADHERENCE rules 6 and 10–12 still describing the retired arc, and the 53 findings across 31 of 41 templates). | 288 | `design/handoff-console-2026-08-21/` (new vendored bundle: README, ADHERENCE, MOBILE, ROUTE_TEMPLATE_MAP, 8 token files, 6 console templates, `_ds_bundle.js`, `component-source/`) + `src/components/ds/` + `scripts/vendor-ds-components.mjs` + `scripts/lint-source.mjs` + `scripts/audit-shell-classes.mjs` + `ci.yml` | **The console handoff, and the mechanism that stops it being re-authored (2026-08-21).** The bundle's README is blunt about the one habit that produces drift — "reading a spec, then writing a fresh `Button` or `Card` that approximates it … **Do not port the components by hand**" — and this repo is the proof: `src/components/TunerDial.tsx` and the design system's `TunerDial` are now two different controls (three stations on the face vs one, step keys inside the lit face vs flanking hardware), both written from the same description months apart, each correct on its day. So the seven console components are **generated** into `src/components/ds/` from the handoff's own `component-source/` by `npm run vendor:ds`, and `npm run guard:ds` (new mandatory CI step) fails if a checked-in file no longer matches what that source produces. An edit there is reverted by the next run, on purpose — a deliberate change belongs in Claude Design and a re-vendored bundle. **The README's own fastest path — a `<script src="_ds_bundle.js">` tag — is not available here, for three reasons that are properties of this app rather than of the bundle:** it is an IIFE reading `React`/`ReactDOM` off `window` (this is RSC inside a Worker: no global React, and no `window` at all on the server); it **executes** its UI kits on load, mounting an `ops` kit into `#root`, so importing it for one knob runs several demo apps; and it is 731KB against a budgeted Worker bundle. `component-source/` is the same code pre-compilation and the README names those files the truth, so generating from them keeps the values literal without a second React runtime. **Four findings, measured not assumed, and all four are DESIGN defects that belong upstream.** (1) The bundle's `tokens/colors.css` re-lightens the four role hues — fan `#8a4fd6`, venue `#0f8f80`, promoter `#c81866`, advertiser `#a5760a` — and every one **fails AA on the cream board** (3.03–4.22:1) where the shipped values clear it (4.50–6.36:1). The shipped tokens are ahead; do not adopt that file. (2) Every component writes type as inline `px`, and 21 sizes sit below the floor the design system itself publishes (ADHERENCE rule 3: 15px content, 11px tracked-mono eyebrow). The generator converts px→rem (`px` cannot follow Settings → Accessibility → Text size, which is applied to the root font size) and raises sub-floor values **to the design system's own floor**, listing every one in `VENDOR_REPORT.md` — a silent raise here is a fix nobody upstream ever hears about. (3) The components hardcode brand colour (`#ff5029`, `rgba(255,80,41,…)`, white sheens) instead of reading their own tokens, so a component cannot follow the ground it is dropped onto; `src/components/ds/` is therefore exempt from `audit:shell`'s colour check for the same reason `audit:design` is advisory — the fix lives in another repository — and the exemption is **not** a silence: all 42 literals are enumerated in the report. (4) `TicketQR` **must never render a real ticket**: its own docstring says it draws a QR-*shaped* matrix from the code string and is "a representation of the door credential, not an encoder", so on a ticket it produces a block no scanner can read. **`lint-source.mjs`'s mono test now also matches `\bfm\b`**, the narrowest change that works: the design system names families through a local alias (`_FP.fm`), so the literal family never appears in the style block the scan reads, and without it a real tracked eyebrow in vendored code is indistinguishable from a sentence and gets raised to the content floor. **No screen was converted, and that is deliberate.** `RotaryNav` is not adoptable as it stands — its cap readout is 8.5px, which the design system's own content floor forbids, and at 15px the word MUSIC does not fit the cap of a 74px knob — and the bundle **disagrees with itself about the nav model**: README replaces the arc nav with that knob while ADHERENCE rule 6 still names the logo trigger and arc as the shell's only chrome. Ripping out the app's only navigation control on the strength of the half of a bundle that contradicts the other half, without the 393px and desktop measurement SHELL_LOCK requires, is how the shell gets broken. Adopting one is now a wrap, not a fork: the shipped `TunerDial` carries three behaviours found by driving the built page — a non-passive `wheel` listener (React's synthetic one is passive, so the design system's own `preventDefault` cannot fire and the page scrolls away under the gesture), roving-tabindex focus restored after the re-render, and a caller-supplied `aria-label` — and those go in a wrapper around the vendored component, never by copying its body to change three lines. | none | ✅ Done (code) — merged in #737 (90ac77a) |
| 287 | `src/app/globals.css`, `mmm.css`, `marketing.css`, `mmm-primitives.css`, `admin/admin.css`, `mmm-workflows.css`, `mobile-fit.css`, `layout.tsx`, `AccessibilityControls.tsx`, `MmmAccessibilitySettings.tsx`, `mmm-me-panels.ts`, ~100 components, `scripts/lint-source.mjs`, `scripts/audit-shell-classes.mjs`, `scripts/audit-mobile.mjs` | **Small text on a phone (2026-08-19, reported: "an older user told me they couldn't read a lot of the smaller text").** Five findings, all measured. **(1) Type was too small in THREE syntaxes, and that is why earlier sweeps missed it.** The 2026-08-12 px-to-rem conversion fixed the JSX `fontSize` prop; it never touched CSS `font-size` (247 px sizes sat in the `<style>` blocks 29 pages embed, plus 52 in the stylesheets, all immune to the Text size setting for exactly the reason that conversion existed) and it could not see the **`font:` shorthand**, which carries the size as a bare length among its other components — 14 more, including the homepage's own "Sign in" link. Every non-eyebrow size now clears **12.5px**, DS8 MOBILE.md's body floor, keeping the tracked mono eyebrow scale (mono family AND letter-spacing >= .14em) exempt down to 9px, tested exactly as `audit:mobile` tests it. **The exemption had leaked onto content** — error messages, hints, form field labels and the "or" divider on the platform's only auth path were shipping at 9-10px because they matched the eyebrow SHAPE. A field label is a control's name, not metadata. `audit:mobile` measures **246 -> 3**. **(2) The DS8 navy repaint left a dead grey behind.** `#5a5048` was secondary copy on the old cream ground and measures **2.39:1** on `#0b1220`; 27 live labels carried it, most at 9-10px. `audit:shell --strict` scans .tsx and still passed it, because its colour check is a lookup of KNOWN literals and a retired value belongs to no token — so a superseded palette goes quiet rather than loud. It now has an entry. **(3) 287 text sites painted below AA.** `--ink-a30` through `--ink-a60` used as a text colour, floored at **`--ink-a65`**. Not a60, which was the obvious answer and is wrong: the light theme's alpha ink is systematically weaker at the same alpha, so a60 measures 4.16-4.47:1 on light cards and fails. a65 clears AA on all eight theme/ground combinations. `--ink-4` (**1.75:1**, a hairline token) was painting the auth card's input placeholder and two labels. The one deliberate exemption is a disabled submit button, commented in place: WCAG exempts disabled controls and raising it would make it look submittable. **(4) The Text size control arrived after first paint.** It was applied in a `useEffect`, so every cold launch painted 100% and jumped — and a cold launch is the normal way into the WebView. Now in the pre-paint bootstrap alongside the theme, with contrast and motion. This also fixed a latent trap: `html.a11y-large-text` set a root `font-size` at (0,1,1) against the scale rule's `:root` at (0,1,0), so turning it on **silently disabled the A-/A+ control entirely**; it contributes a custom property now and the three scales multiply, capped at 1.6. **(5) iOS never got the reader's system text size.** Android's WebView scales web text with the OS font setting; **WKWebView does not** — Dynamic Type does not reach CSS — so a reader who had already enlarged text system-wide got nothing from us and no reason to suspect an in-app control existed. Bridged with an `-apple-system-body` probe against its 17px default. Three guards, each load-bearing: `CSS.supports` gates it to engines that parse the keyword (elsewhere it computes to the inherited 16px and a naive ratio would **shrink** the app 6%), `Math.max(1, ...)` makes it a floor and never a shrink (which also disposes of desktop Safari, where it computes to 13px and means nothing), and the cap rides with the multiply. Re-probed on resume, since a reader changes it in Settings and comes back to a page that never reloaded. **All four checks are now ratchets rather than a one-off sweep**: `lint-source.mjs` fails on any of the three type syntaxes below the floor **and now walks .css at all** (it collected only .ts/.tsx/.js/.mjs, so a third of the debt was outside every check it ran), and `audit:mobile`'s budget drops 246 -> 6. Measured against a local production build at 393x852; 27 Satori/OG and print surfaces stay px by design. **Not done, and deliberately:** the one-tap-from-ME goal for the control. ME's two canonical panels are a prior design decision and adding a third is DS8's call, not this change's — Accessibility is now first inside Settings instead. | ✅ Done (code) — merged in #727 (d276553) |

A bare `1fr` is `minmax(auto, 1fr)`, and `auto` floors a track at its cell's min-content width — the event links inside set `white-space: nowrap`, so their min-content is the **full untruncated show title** and the `text-overflow: ellipsis` beside it never gets the chance to fire. One long title widens its column and takes the whole seven-column grid sideways with it. Measured in Chromium at 393px against the real cell and link styles, with a 62-character title: **558px of grid scrollWidth inside a 361px container, pushing the document to 574px** — 181px of horizontal page overflow. With the floor at 0 it measures 361px and the ellipsis works. Applied to the other 15 content grids in the same pass because the construct is identical and `minmax(0, 1fr)` only ever *lowers* a floor — it cannot change a grid that already fits, and there is no reason to wait for each one to be reported. **`.adv-dot-grid` in `AdvertisePage.tsx` is deliberately excluded**: it lays out dots, not text, so `auto` can never exceed its track and the bare `1fr` is correct there. **Two things this turned up that are NOT defects and should not be "fixed" by a later session:** the `camera` primer is unwired because the door scanner at `/shows/[slug]/scan` is a text input with no camera in it — the primer is correctly waiting on a scanner that does not exist — and `wallet`/`share`/`offlineTickets` carry `prompts: false` on purpose, being documentation of a fallback rather than a gate. | 284 | `design/design-system-8/templates/simplified-app/map.html` — the date strip | **MAP's date strip, the last of the three shell surfaces (3 of 3, after rows 282-283).** Five day pills and a summary row, on the EVENTS layer only — an event has a date and a venue or an artist does not, which is also why `dates` is never sent to those endpoints. **It is a SET of days, not a span, and that is the whole design.** The map document is explicit: *"Clicking the strip toggles one day, so a Friday and a Sunday with nothing between them is a legal selection — which is what anyone planning a weekend actually wants."* Every consequence follows from that, including the summary line, which says `Aug 7 – Aug 9 · 2 days selected` rather than `· 3 days` when the run has a gap — describing it as a range would claim a Saturday nobody picked. With nothing selected it reads **"Any day"**, not "None": the strip is a filter, and an unset filter is not an empty result. **Server side, `GET /api/map/events` grew a `dates` param** — comma-separated `YYYY-MM-DD`, capped at 31, each becoming a half-open `[00:00, next 00:00)` window. Absent or unparseable means no filter, so an older client keeps working. This is what makes the design's other rule true — a venue with nothing booked on the chosen days drops OFF the map rather than sitting there as a dead pin. **A real bug was introduced and caught in the same pass**: the date windows were first added as a second top-level `OR`, and `genre` already uses one — in an object literal the later spread silently replaces the earlier, so a genre-filtered map would have ignored the date strip entirely, with no error. They are combined under `AND` now. **Dates are LOCAL calendar days on both sides.** `toDayKey` formats from `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate`, never `toISOString()`, which converts to UTC first and makes anyone east of Greenwich after 5pm ask for tomorrow. The server resolves the same string against its own zone, so someone asking for Friday gets Friday *where the show is* — the one they mean. Day arithmetic uses `setDate`, not `+86_400_000`, so a DST boundary shifts the clock rather than landing on the same calendar day twice in the autumn. 13 tests on the pure helpers (`src/lib/map-dates.ts`), covering the local-vs-UTC boundary, month and leap-day crossings, the non-contiguous selection, and the gap case in the summary. **Deliberately NOT built: the CALENDAR button** the mockup shows beside the summary. It opens a mini-calendar whose two-click span-picking writes into the same set, and shipping the button without the popover would be a control that does nothing — the same failure as a chip that navigates nowhere. The strip covers the next five days; the calendar is what reaches past them, and it is the remaining piece of this surface. | `GET /api/map/events` (+ `dates`) | ✅ |
| 283 | `design/design-system-8/templates/simplified-app/` — the ME pane | **ME rebuilt as the design's four accordions (2 of 3, after row 282's player).** The surface was a flat stack of cards; the design is `Your year` · `My tickets` · `About me` · `Profiles`, each a labelled button carrying `aria-expanded`, a chevron and `--radius-card` corners. **Why accordions and not four cards**: ME is the only surface in this shell with no search and no tabs, so everything an account has lives on one scroll — left open, three screens of stats sit between the member and the Account rows, which are what most visits are for. "Your year" opens by default because it is the only section that is not an errand. The chevron is ONE element rotated rather than two glyphs, so it cannot render a direction that disagrees with `aria-expanded`. **The add-profile entries are new and real.** `POST /api/profiles` already creates a profile for a signed-in account and `PagesHome.tsx` already calls it, so `Add artist profile` / `Add venue profile` link to `/pages?create=…` rather than growing a second creator. **The `Verified` chip is not decoration** — both types go through the verification queue all three onboarding wizards promise a 48-hour turnaround on. **Fan is deliberately absent**: every account already is one, implicit and permanent (`BACKEND_REWRITE.md` §1). The design's promoter sentence is carried verbatim — *"Promoting is not a profile"* — which is the same rule `CLAUDE.md` states and the reason there is no promoter role picker anywhere. **Nothing was invented to fill a section.** `My tickets` stays a bridge to `/tickets` and `/shows`: this shell has no events module (the arc carries MAP, MUSIC and ME and nothing else), and a second tickets UI here would be a copy to keep in sync. The bridge itself is load-bearing — since row 273 pointed the legacy nav INTO this shell, a member arriving via MAP had no first-class route to their own tickets. Section mapping follows source order so no data moved: `Your year` = the stat grid, `Profiles` = the page card plus the new entries, `About me` = the HYPE link and recent activity. **Still to come: MAP's date strip** (3 of 3). | `POST /api/profiles` (existing) | ✅ |

The shell's structure was already correct"* — and shipped a colour/type re-anchor on that basis. **"Structure" there meant the architecture** (map as base layer, module routes, where things mount), **not component fidelity.** `SHELL_LOCK_2026-08-08.md` is a detailed component spec, it was read and quoted into `CLAUDE.md` as "signed off as final", and nothing checked whether the code implemented it. It did not. This also sat squarely in the gap flagged in rows 275-277 and never closed: the signed-in shells cannot be measured without a session fixture and a database, so every audit stopped at the signed-out pages. **The player was a 42px CIRCLE showing a letter, a title, one heart and a play button.** Against the lock: 64px **squircle** artwork (`anchorHeight - 24`, radius `round(64 × 0.342)` — the square holds the artist's *logo* and a circle crops a mark), one row with the artwork as first child so the scrub row aligns with the title **by construction** (the prototype's `paddingLeft: 58` only lined up at one artwork size), a **continuous** accent hairline rather than a partial bar (which competed with the scrub row and hung past the capsule at each end), volume fixed at **108px** with seek taking the remainder (volume was unaimable at 64), and marquee only on overflow — measuring the FIRST copy, since the duplicate only exists once overflow is true and halving `scrollWidth` on the first pass concludes nothing ever overflows. **HYPE and favourite are now two controls, because they are two acts.** The lock: collapsing them into one heart "quietly removed the mechanic the product is named after" — which is exactly what shipped. | 281 | (no design source — GitHub Pages build failure pasted by the user) | **The failing build was the only thing keeping this repository off the public internet, so the fix is NOT to make it pass.** GitHub Pages is enabled in the repo *settings* — there is no Pages workflow in `.github/workflows/` — so GitHub runs `actions/jekyll-build-pages` against the repo root on every push. It has been dying on `Liquid Exception: Variable '{{ animationDelay: ` + '`' + `${i * 70}' was not properly terminated` + '`' + ` in `design/handoff-music-map-me/FRONTEND_GOTCHAS.md`. Jekyll renders every `.md` as a Liquid template (`jekyll-optional-front-matter`, which the log shows loaded), and **17 markdown files** carry `{{ … }}` — JSX inline styles inside code fences, which Liquid parses anyway. **The obvious fix — `{% raw %}` across those 17 files — would have been actively harmful.** A green Jekyll build *publishes this repository as a website* at the org's github.io address: `CLAUDE.md` (Supabase host `db.bjkabtzvgfshsrmjhrkx…`, the Hyperdrive id, the KV namespace id, the R2 bucket), `DEPLOY.md`, `SECURITY_COMPLIANCE.md`, `DESIGN_SYNC.md` and the entire vendored design system. None of it is a credential; all of it is a map of the infrastructure. The Liquid error has been the accidental guard. Making CI green would have switched on a public site nobody asked for — the exact failure mode of treating "fix all errors" as "turn the tick green". So `_config.yml` publishes **nothing**: `include: []`, every one of the 45 tracked top-level paths listed in `exclude:`, and a `published: false` default so a path that escapes the list is still not a page. Nothing left to render means nothing left to fail. `.nojekyll` added alongside it. **The real fix is a repository setting** — Settings → Pages → Source: None — which is the operator's to make; this is the safety net for as long as it is on, and is worth keeping afterwards so re-enabling Pages by hand or by accident cannot start publishing again. **Guarded against drift** by `src/lib/__tests__/pages-exclusion.test.ts`: a list of paths rots, and the failure mode here is silent by construction — add a top-level directory, forget the list, and the next build publishes it with no error, because the entire point of the change was to stop the build erroring. The test walks `git ls-files`, asserts every non-dot top-level entry is excluded, names the four most damaging files explicitly so removing one fails here rather than in a security review, and checks the two fail-safe defaults. 3 tests. | — | ✅ |
| 280 | (no design source — admin hardening, explicit user request: "Send admin creation to admin@ihype.org and create a rule that only allows that") | **ADMIN is now granted by the row and ALLOWED by the address on it.** `isAdminSession()` asked one question — `role === 'ADMIN'` — so any `User` row that acquired that value was an administrator: by an operator's hand, a bad migration, a restored backup, or a compromised endpoint. `POST /api/admin/setup` has always hardcoded `admin@ihype.org` when it *creates* the account; nothing stopped a different row from *holding* the role afterwards. **The rule lives in `src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts`** — dependency-light (no `@/lib/db`, no `next/*`) so the auth callbacks, route handlers and tests can all import it. Addresses are normalised before comparison, because a rule that treats `ADMIN@iHYPE.org` and `admin@ihype.org` differently fails open the first time somebody capitalises their own address. **A missing address is refused, not ignored** — `User.email` is nullable and the passkey signup path collects none, so "no email" must not read as "not denied". `ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS` can widen it, read through `readRuntimeEnv` at the call site rather than captured at module load — the mistake `ADMIN_EMAIL` made, where `process.env` at import time is empty in a Worker and the const silently resolved to its default forever. An empty or unparseable value falls back to the default rather than to an empty allowlist, which would lock out the person trying to fix it. **Enforced in the jwt callback, not in the helper.** `token.role` is the one value every consumer reads, and grepping found two routes (`stripe/connect/onboard`, `stripe/connect/refresh`) comparing `session.user.role` directly rather than going through `isAdminSession()` — patching the helper alone would have left them. `clampAdminRole()` downgrades ADMIN to FAN when the stored address is not allowlisted, and runs on **every** `auth()` call rather than only at sign-in, for the same reason `securityVersion` does: a token minted before the rule existed must stop being an admin token without waiting to expire. The database is not rewritten — this is an authorisation rule, not a migration, and silently editing roles would destroy the evidence that one was wrong. Refusals `log.error` to Sentry. `isAdminSession()` keeps a second, synchronous email check so the rule does not depend on which path built the `Session`. **Admin creation now tells the mailbox that owns it.** Creating the administrator is the most sensitive thing this deployment can do and it happened in silence — an audit row nobody reads unless they already suspect something. A notice now goes to `admin@ihype.org` itself, so whoever holds it learns an admin account exists even if they did not run setup; it names the requesting IP and says to claim the passkey immediately and set `ALLOW_ADMIN_SETUP=false` if unexpected. Deferred via `deferWork` and not awaited — a mail outage must not fail the one endpoint that can bootstrap access — with the audit row written first as the durable record. **⚠️ This locks out the personal Gmail address** that `CLAUDE.md` then documented as the current ADMIN (the address itself was removed from this repo on 2026-08-14 — `admin@ihype.org` is the only email this product uses). That is the request, not an oversight, and the recovery path already exists and targets exactly the allowlisted address: `POST /api/admin/setup` with `ALLOW_ADMIN_SETUP=true` + `ADMIN_SETUP_SECRET` creates or claims `admin@ihype.org` and issues a one-time passkey link. **Order matters** — `invite_only_signup` is still off (rows 277-279) and lives behind `/admin`, so flip it BEFORE deploying this, or the flag becomes unreachable from a signed-in session. 20 new tests. | — | ✅ |
| 279 | (no design source — alpha reset tooling, explicit user request: "I promise you they're not real. Clear all users.") | **A tested, dry-run-first wipe of every account, and the two traps a plain `DELETE FROM "User"` walks into.** The user confirmed the 7 artists / 3 fans on the live landing page (row 278) are seed data, not real signups, and asked for them gone. **This session has no write path to production** — the Supabase MCP is disconnected, is pinned `read_only=true`, and `apply_migration` is de-allowlisted — so `scripts/clear-all-users.mjs` is the deliverable and the operator runs it. Deliberately NOT a migration: anything in `prisma/migrations/` auto-deploys on merge, and CLAUDE.md records that three "DO NOT APPLY BLIND" migrations shipped exactly that way. A destructive one-off belongs behind a confirmation phrase, not behind a comment. **Trap 1 — `ON DELETE RESTRICT`.** 30 of the FKs pointing at `User` cascade and 6 SET NULL, but `Ad.advertiserId` is RESTRICT and raises, aborting the delete partway. The migration history holds a second (`CollabResponse`) for a model no longer in `schema.prisma`, so a hand-written list of blockers would be wrong in one direction or the other. One transaction; a partial wipe is worse than none. **Verified against a real database, not reasoned about.** Local Postgres 16, all 109 migrations applied, production-shaped seed: dry run changed nothing and previewed the cascade; the default run deleted 3 users, cascaded 3 profiles away and kept the ADMIN; `KEEP_ADMINS=0` reached 0 users; a `CAPTURED` order refused with exit 1. | 264 | (no design source — anti-bot ticket purchasing, explicit user request) | **The purchase endpoint looked defended and was not.** It required login, a verified email and 18+, capped quantity at 8, and rate-limited to 10 orders/hour — but `rateLimitKey(prefix, userId, ip)` returns `prefix:user:<id>` whenever a session exists and **silently discards the `ip` argument**, which 13 call sites pass. Purchase always has a session, so the address was never counted: one machine driving fifty throwaway accounts got fifty independent buckets — **4,000 tickets an hour entirely within the stated limit**. The 8 was also per *order*, not per person, so a single account could legitimately take 80 tickets for one show in an hour. **Three layers added, cheapest-friction first.** (1) New `consumeDualRateLimit()` counts the account AND the client address as two independent buckets, both of which must pass — deliberately not one combined `user+ip` key, which would be worse than useless since rotating either half mints a fresh bucket. The IP limit is looser (25/hr vs 10/hr) because a household, a venue's wifi and a corporate NAT all legitimately present one address; a null address skips the IP bucket rather than lumping every unidentifiable client into one shared counter that one of them could exhaust for all the others. `rateLimitKey` keeps its behaviour (correct for most endpoints) but now documents the trap in its own doc comment. (2) **Per-account, per-show cap of 8** — the user's decision — counted across every non-VOID order including still-RESERVED ones, since a bot that opens reservations and never captures would otherwise hold an allocation while counting as zero. (3) **Turnstile on every purchase** — the user's decision — reusing the existing widget and verifier from registration, checked *before* any Stripe customer is created or inventory touched, and failing closed in production. **The residual race is documented rather than hidden:** the cap counts then inserts inside one ReadCommitted transaction, so two simultaneous requests can observe the same total. It is not the primary defence — the rate-limit buckets are atomic (Durable Object) and bound a burst regardless of who wins. Escalating to Serializable would be the *wrong* fix: every buyer for a show writes the same `Show` row, so on-sale traffic would abort constantly. Closing it fully needs a per-(buyer, show) aggregate to guard with the same conditional `updateMany` the capacity check already uses — a migration, so it belongs in `migrations-pending`. **Account age is advisory only, never blocking**: refusing a fan who signed up minutes ago because their favourite band just announced a date would punish the single most common legitimate reason to create an iHYPE account. It logs to Sentry so an attack is visible instead of silent. **Client**: the Turnstile token is cleared and the widget reset after *every* completed submit, because Turnstile tokens are single-use — without that, a buyer who hit the cap or had a card declined could not retry, since the second submit would replay a spent token and be refused as a bot. The submit button gates on a token only when a site key is configured, matching `AuthRegister`, so checkout cannot deadlock in an environment without one. Decision logic is pure and tested (`src/lib/ticket-purchase-guard.ts`, 12 tests) per the `player-recovery`/`focus-trap` precedent. **Five new route tests each assert a refusal, and were negative-controlled**: neutering the bot check fails 1, neutering the cap fails 2 — a guard nothing proves refuses is decoration. tsc clean, vitest 469/469 (17 new), lint clean (714 files), `next build` clean. | POST /api/shows/[showId]/tickets (accepts `turnstileToken`; new 400 `BOT_CHECK_FAILED`, 409 `TICKET_LIMIT_REACHED`, 429 `RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After`) | ✅ Done — **needs `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` set in production or the check fails closed and blocks all purchases** |

Six hardcoded values swapped for tokens; verified by computed style in both themes (light `rgb(26,20,16)` on `rgba(245,242,237,.94)`, dark `rgb(240,235,229)` on `rgba(10,8,5,.94)`). This is chrome-contract rule 8 — never hardcode a colour — being violated by code that predates the shell, and it was invisible until something rendered the dock on a light background. **Also unified the skeleton**, backlog item 5's other half: it existed under three names (`.skeleton`, `.ihype-skeleton`, and an inline `Shimmer` in `src/app/shows/loading.tsx` whose hex fallback `#1a1712` is not the token it claims to fall back from), the same fragmentation the nine primitives had. | 262 | (no design source — accessibility coverage for the signed-in shell, explicit user request) | **The signed-in product had never been through axe, and when it finally was, both themes failed.** `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts` only ever audited public pages, so the entire app shell was unaudited while the suite stayed green. New `e2e/app-shell-a11y.spec.ts` runs axe over six shell routes plus the open drawer — **and over both themes**, which turned out to be the point: headless Chromium reports `prefers-color-scheme: light`, so the first run silently audited only the non-canonical theme and every failure it found was a light value.
