Two **text-only** tokens added rather than touching brand fills: `--accent-text` (the accent itself on dark, `#9c2707` on light) and `--role-venue-text` (`#0b6f67` on light) — the venue teal measures 10.6:1 on the dark charter card but **1.26:1** on the light one, because that card's surface *is* the same teal at 10% over a light page, so colour and background converge. Fills everywhere keep `--accent`/`--role-venue`. **The spec reports measured-vs-required ratios and the actual fg/bg**, not just a selector list, because for contrast that ratio *is* the fix instruction — a bare `expected []` sends the reader to a JSON artifact. Result: **14/14 green across both themes**, and the four pre-existing marketing failures on `/login`/`/register`/`/discover` are down to one (`.nav-join-button`, white on the brand accent at 3.27:1 — a genuine brand-vs-AA tension that needs a design decision, not a unilateral token change). **Also settled a question rather than assuming:** `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts` fails, and rather than call it pre-existing a second time I checked out the pre-session commit, rebuilt, and ran it — **7/7 fail there versus 2/7 now**, so that suite was already broken and this session's changes improved it. tsc clean, vitest 452/452, lint clean (712 files), app-shell contract 16/16, app-shell a11y 14/14. | (no API change) | ✅ Done — `.nav-join-button` contrast still needs a design call |
| 261 | (no design source — e2e coverage for the app-shell contract, explicit user request) | **The chrome contract is now executable, and running it found four real bugs — three of them mine.** Rows 258–260 rested on comments and on where one component happened to be mounted; nothing failed if a rule broke. `e2e/app-shell.spec.ts` asserts all of it against the real app under workerd: the geometry table, rule 2 (`html`/`body` `overflow:hidden`, content region `position:absolute`/`overflow:auto`), rules 3+4 (auto-hide at the 24px threshold, mobile only, restored at the top, never on desktop), rules 5+6 (drawer/scrim edges, 308px, content unmoved because an overlay reserves no space), the drawer (logo tile is the only affordance and mirrors `aria-expanded`, focus trap survives 25 Tabs, Escape returns focus to the tile, scrim dismisses, accordion opens the owning section and only that one), the manifest's role gates, `aria-current` on the strip, and scope (marketing keeps marketing chrome; `/` redirects a signed-in member into the shell; signed-out gets no shell). **Rule 1 finally has a test** — tag the live header and content nodes, navigate, assert the same nodes are still there; moving `AppShell` into a route-group layout would now fail loudly instead of silently. **16 tests, all verified passing against a real workerd instance and a real Postgres in this sandbox**, not written-and-hoped. **The suite does not depend on the `TEST_SESSION_COOKIE` secret.** Every other authenticated spec `test.skip()`s without it; for a contract suite that would mean its own greenness was the lie, so `e2e/fixtures/session.ts` seeds a user (plus creator profiles for the role gates) and signs its own cookie, mirroring `buildAuthSessionCookie`'s token shape — the claim that matters is `securityVersion`, which `auth()`'s jwt callback re-reads from the row on every call. Building it against a real DB immediately caught four API errors that reading alone would not (`datasources` → `datasourceUrl` → Prisma 7's required driver adapter; `User.username` and `Profile.hexId`/`genres` being non-null). **Four bugs the tests found:** (1) **`scripts/e2e-workerd.mjs` spawned `npx.cmd`** — a Windows-only shim — so the entire authenticated e2e suite died with ENOENT on any Linux host, including CI. Combined with the `vars.E2E_ENABLED` gate, it had evidently never run. (2) **Row 260's scroll fix only worked half the time**: it keyed on `pathname`, but the context strip's siblings differ only by `?tab=`, which is the most common navigation in the whole shell — the test expected 0 and got 141. Moved to `ShellScrollManager`, keyed on the full URL. (3) **Back-restore restored the wrong number, and the cause was not where it looked.** Sampling every frame showed the restore *working* and faithfully returning to **77** — so the bug was in the capture: by the time the route-change effect read `scrollTop`, the browser had already moved the region. Position is now recorded continuously on scroll, and the restore happens *inside* the `popstate` handler rather than from a flag it sets, because measured ordering is `[set:900, set:0, set:0, popstate]` — the router re-renders before the event ever arrives, so any flag is read too early. (4) **Focus never returned to the logo tile**: the check lived in the effect cleanup, and React detaches `panelRef` before a cleanup for an unmounting subtree runs, so it always read null. Dismissal is now decided at close time, and only for Escape/scrim — selecting a row navigates, and stealing focus back would undo the move. **Also:** three of my own test bugs, fixed rather than worked around (`.shell-root` has a zero-height box *by design*, so `toBeAttached`; the scrim click at x:10 landed on the 308px drawer; and `/` redirects a signed-in member, so that test's premise was wrong). `npm run audit:shell -- --strict` is wired into CI now that drift is zero. **Pre-existing and NOT caused by this work:** `e2e/accessibility.spec.ts` fails colour-contrast on `/login`, `/register`, `/discover` and a show page — every violation targets marketing chrome (`.nav-join-button`, `.ihype-cookie-consent`, `.ihype-btn-ghost`, the privacy link) on signed-out pages where the shell never mounts; zero shell classes appear in any report. tsc clean, vitest 452/452, lint clean (712 files), `next build` clean, 16/16 e2e green. | (no API change) | ✅ Done — **CI still needs `vars.E2E_ENABLED` set to `true` for any of this to run there** |
| 260 | (no design source — pre-alpha hardening of rows 258/259, explicit user request) | **Five defects in the shell, two of which I had introduced. Found by auditing the seams the shell created rather than by waiting for reports.** **(1) The content region never reset its scroll on navigation.** The chrome contract makes `.shell-content` the scroll container, but Next's scroll handling moves the *window* — which no longer scrolls. Scroll halfway down Charts, open Near Me, and you land halfway down a page you have never seen. It fired on **every** navigation. Fixed with real scroll management, not just a reset: forward navigation goes to the top or to the `#hash` target (the bell's `/me/dashboard#notifications` depends on it), while Back/Forward restores the remembered offset per URL, driven by a `popstate` flag — losing your place on Back is the same bug pointing the other way. **(2) The text-size setting was not what the handoff asked for.** It was a `zoom` on the content wrapper, which is a transform in all but name: it disturbs `position: fixed` descendants (every modal in the app) and scaled layout rather than type. The handoff says "root font scale, **not a transform**", so it is now `:root { font-size: calc(100% * var(--ihype-text-scale)) }` — `100%` rather than a px base so the reader's own browser font-size is multiplied, not overridden — and the shell's content type was converted to `rem` so it actually follows. **The chrome deliberately did not convert**: the header/strip/drawer/player are sized in px precisely because the geometry table depends on them not moving. Verified across 0.85/1.0/1.4 in headless Chromium: root 13.6→16→22.4px with page h1 27.2→32→44.8px and stat labels, row titles and the back pill all tracking, while header 82px, strip 52px, drawer 308px, logo 48px and the chrome's own type never budged. The stat value needed its `clamp()` converted too — a px clamp ignores the root scale, so it was the one element left behind. **(3) Modal scroll-lock had become a no-op.** `acquireScrollLock()` locks `html`/`body`, which no longer scroll inside the shell, so every overlay in the app (report dialog, fan mail, radio composer, quick grid) left the page scrolling behind it. The ref-counted helper is untouched; the class it already sets now also reaches the element that actually scrolls, at every width — modals are not a phone-only affordance. **(4) The drawer had no focus management at all** — no trap, no focus return, no `role="dialog"` — so a keyboard or screen-reader user tabbed straight past the scrim into content they could not see, on a design whose README leads on AT affordances. Now `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"`, focus moves in on open and returns to the logo tile on close **only if it is still inside the drawer** (selecting a row navigates, and yanking focus back afterwards would undo the move the user just made). **The decision is a pure function** in `src/lib/focus-trap.ts` with 8 tests, following the `player-recovery.ts` precedent, because trapping fails in two directions and there is no DOM test environment in this repo to catch either. The test that matters covers the case a first-/last-only implementation misses entirely: **a scrim click leaves `activeElement` on `<body>`**, so no edge condition matches and the next Tab escapes — the trap now pulls focus back to whichever edge the user is heading for. **(5) `getShellViewer()` ran three uncached queries in the root layout on every navigation** for every signed-in page view; wrapped in React's `cache()`. **Deliberately not done, and stated rather than quietly skipped:** the sparse-content pass (backlog item 5 — this design assumes density and alpha will not have it), localisation beyond chrome (backlog 7 — the Language card offers 12 locales and only the chrome is translated), the narrow-viewport design (backlog 6), the three per-route layouts row 259 held back, and whether the back pill should be contextual rather than always "For you". Those need product/design decisions, not code. tsc clean, vitest 452/452 (8 new), lint clean (711 files), full `next build` clean. | (no API change) | ✅ Done |
| 259 | `iHYPE App Shell.dc.html` §6–20 (route structure + copy) + screenshots 02/05/06/07 | **Converted all 63 subpages inside the shell to the design system — as a stylesheet, on purpose.** Row 258 built the chrome; the routes inside it still looked like twenty unrelated products. The cause was measurable rather than aesthetic: **607 distinct class names across 63 pages, of which ~215 are one of nine primitives wearing a different prefix** — nine separate names for StatCard alone (`promo-stat-card`, `pa-stat-card`, `fan-dash-stat-card`, `ad-dash-stat-card`, `aa-stat-card`, `fa-stat-card`, `ad-stat-card`, `venue-stat`, `stat`), 30 for Card, 22 for Row, 22 for EmptyState. The handoff describes exactly that small vocabulary, so `src/app/shell-surfaces.css` defines each primitive **once** from the handoff's own values and points every existing name at it. **Rewriting 63 components was considered and rejected**: the markup already has the right shape, so re-authoring it would have put every auth gate and Prisma query on those pages at risk for no visual gain — the opposite of the rule at the top of CLAUDE.md. **The mechanism is specificity, not luck.** 29 of the 63 pages carry their own inline `<style>` block, which Next.js emits *after* any stylesheet, so document order was always going to favour the page. Scoping every selector under `.shell-content` scores (0,2,0) against the page's own (0,1,0), and specificity outranks order — so the conversion lands without deleting a single page's CSS, and nothing outside the signed-in shell is touched. Nine pages style their heading through a descendant selector (`.promo-page-header h1`, `.fan-dash-header h1`, `.settings-page h1`) which ties the element ramp at (0,1,1); those are prefixed `.shell-root` to (0,2,1) to settle it. **Verified against real markup, not a mock**: rendered `/me/promote`'s and `/community`'s verbatim JSX *together with their own extracted inline `<style>` blocks loaded last*, in headless Chromium, and asserted the computed styles — stat card `--bg-surface`/12px/centred with a Syne 800 `--accent` value and a mono uppercase `--ink-2` label, rows at 12px on `--bg-surface`, h1 at 32px Syne, gradient CTAs replaced by the design's radius-8 solid accent, page containers unnested from their own max-widths. **The card-vs-section distinction is the subtle part and it works**: a heading *inside* a card or row types 15px/500 DM Sans (the handoff says so explicitly for Community/Info/Legal — "title 15px + body 13.5px/1.5" — and every screenshot agrees), while a heading *outside* one keeps the Syne 800 ramp; confirmed live at 15px vs 26px on the same page. **Deliberately paint-only** — no `display`, `grid-template` or `position` — because those carry each page's real layout and overriding them from a central sheet would break pages sight-unseen. **Two by-name assumptions were wrong and were caught by checking rather than shipping:** `.community-cta` looks like a button by its name but is a flex *panel* containing an `h2` and a `p` (it would have become an inline accent button), and `stat-sub` carries a descriptive sentence here rather than the handoff's short `+38%` delta, so the 10px uppercase accent treatment turned a sentence into a wrapped orange ribbon — it is quiet supporting copy now, with an opt-in `.is-delta` for the real delta form. Also added: the back pill (`‹ For you`), rendered by the shell rather than by 63 pages because no page knows what it is a sub-route of — and it is deliberately *not* `history.back()`, since the handoff shows the same "For you" label on every route, making it a fixed way home. **New `npm run audit:shell`** (`scripts/audit-shell-classes.mjs`) reports primitive-shaped classes that are not converted and aliases matching no markup, so a new page cannot silently drift back; currently zero unconverted. | 257 | (no design source — music player audit + fixes, explicit user request) | **The player was well built but had one gap, and that gap was live.** `GlobalMediaPlayer.tsx` already does the hard parts properly: Media Session API with `setPositionState` (lock-screen art, scrub, hardware controls), a real second `<audio preload="auto">` that warms the next URL and only hands off at `readyState >= 2`, shuffle/repeat/volume/playback-rate, localStorage persistence, 15 aria-labels, `play()` rejection caught for mobile autoplay policy, and `MediaListen` recorded via `upsert` so replays don't double-count. **What was missing: any `error` listener on the audio element.** It registered `timeupdate`, `loadedmetadata` and `ended` only — so a 404, CORS rejection or undecodable file left the UI showing "playing" with the position frozen and the queue never advancing. It stops when the queue holds one track, at the end with repeat off, when every track in a short queue has failed, and — deliberately — under `repeat: 'one'`, which would otherwise re-request the same dead URL forever; a long queue is capped at 5 consecutive skips. **Seed track unpublished** via `20260729030000_unpublish_dangling_seed_track`, an **UPDATE not a DELETE** so it is reversible in one statement and the `hexId` stays stable for existing `/tracks/<hexId>` and `/embed/<hexId>` links; `prisma/launch-seed.ts` now sets `isPublished: false` on both create and update so re-seeding cannot resurrect it. A catalogue of 100% broken links reads worse than an empty one. tsc clean, vitest 373/373 (9 new), lint clean (698 files), gated-migration guard clean, production build compiles. | (no API change — `/api/media-listens` unchanged) | ✅ Done (code + reversible migration) |

| 255 | (no design source — root-cause follow-up to row 254) | **Worker secrets were invisible to two subsystems, which is why both reported themselves unconfigured.** Reading the app's own last-ever email (Resend `get-email` on the 2026-06-23 "Launch readiness blockers" message) showed exactly two blockers — `Set STRIPE_SECRET_KEY to a valid sk_ secret.` and `Set STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET…` — and **no email blockers**, i.e. `EMAIL_FROM`/`RESEND_API_KEY` were readable that day (it sent `From: no-reply@ihype.org`) while the Stripe values were not. **The pattern:** on workerd, `process.env` carries build values and `wrangler.toml [vars]`, but Worker *secrets* only exist on the Cloudflare env binding. Every module that needed one had already grown its own fallback — `db.ts:57` (`process.env[name] || readCloudflareEnv(name)`), `cron-auth.ts` (`CRON_SECRET`), plus `r2.ts`/`ai.ts`/`analytics.ts` reading bindings directly — and the only two that never did are `src/lib/env.ts` (RESEND_API_KEY, EMAIL_FROM) and `src/lib/payments.ts` (STRIPE_*): precisely the two subsystems reporting "not configured". Crons kept running throughout (row 254 proved it via `show-lifecycle`) **because cron-auth has the fallback**. **Built:** `src/lib/runtime-env.ts` — `readRuntimeEnv(name)`, `runtimeEnvSource()`, `hasRuntimeEnvContext()`. Purely additive: a value already in `process.env` always wins, so nothing working today can regress. `runtimeEnvSource()` **drops non-string entries on purpose** — the Cloudflare env also holds bindings (KV/R2/DO/AI), and handing an object to a `z.string()` schema would turn a working deploy into a hard `Server misconfiguration` throw. `payments.ts` now reads all three values through it; `env.ts` parses the merged source and **only memoises once a request context exists**, since the previous unconditional cache could pin every secret to `undefined` for the whole isolate if first touched at module init or build. Also added `getAdminEmail()` — `ADMIN_EMAIL` was resolved at module scope, before any context, so a configured `ADMIN_ALERT_EMAIL` could never have been seen (the old const is kept and marked deprecated rather than removed). 7 new tests via a documented reader seam (the lookup is a synchronous `require()`, matching `db.ts`, which vitest cannot intercept) covering: a secret visible only on the binding is found, `process.env` still wins, blank values fall through, no-context returns undefined instead of throwing, bindings never reach zod, and existing values are never overwritten. **What this does and does not prove:** it does **not** establish that the Stripe key exists in Cloudflare — only `wrangler secret list` can. It removes the ambiguity: with the fallback in place, if the secret is set the buy button stops being hidden behind "Paid tickets · Coming soon", and if it is genuinely absent the readiness blocker is now trustworthy rather than an artefact of where the code looked. tsc clean, vitest 364/364 (7 new), lint clean (697 files), production build succeeds. | (no API change) | ✅ Done (code) — **operator must still run `wrangler secret list` to confirm STRIPE_SECRET_KEY / STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET / EMAIL_FROM actually exist** |
| 254 | (no design source — second production-health audit, user request: "keep digging, I need this app to run flawlessly at alpha launch") | **Production has sent zero emails for 35 days and nothing noticed.** Verified against Resend's own API logs, not inferred: the last `POST /emails` from the app was **2026-06-23 18:01**; every request after that is this session's MCP calls. Until then the daily ops report, DB health alerts and cron-failure alerts arrived every day. **Crons are NOT the cause** — proved by a transition only a cron performs: both `Show` rows are `ENDED` with `startsAt` of 2026-06-30 and 2026-07-07, i.e. `show-lifecycle` ran well after the email cutoff. So jobs execute and email specifically fails. **Why it was invisible — three compounding silences, all the same shape as the P3009 outage:** (1) every operational send was written `sendGenericEmail(...).catch(() => {})` — six sites, including `daily-ops` (which sends unconditionally every day) and the `health-check` job itself; (2) the alert paths inside `health-check` are each guarded on `isEmailDeliveryConfigured()`, so a misconfigured mailer turns the whole job into a silent no-op rather than an error; (3) `/api/cron/report-failure` authenticates with **the same `CRON_SECRET`** as the jobs it monitors (`isCronRequestAuthorized`), so any credential drift silences the jobs and the alarm about the jobs together. In every case the monitor was downstream of the thing monitored. **Fixed:** new `sendOperationalEmail(input, context)` in `src/lib/mailer.ts` — never throws at the caller, but reports failures through `log.error` → **Sentry, an independent channel**, since email cannot be what tells you email is down; all six swallow sites converted; `health-check` now emits a Sentry error naming `getEmailDeliveryReadiness().blockers` when delivery is unconfigured; `daily-ops` returns `emailed` in its JSON so a 200 no longer implies delivery. **Root cause not provable from here:** `isEmailDeliveryConfigured()` requires `RESEND_API_KEY && EMAIL_FROM`, and `EMAIL_FROM` has **never** appeared in `wrangler.toml` in the full git history (only `.env.example`), while `wrangler deploy` runs without `--keep-vars` and so replaces the Worker's plain vars with the file's on every deploy. Deliberately did **not** guess the value into production config; the next `health-check` run now prints the exact blocker. **Also found:** `AuxQueue` and `PromoCode` are read by `/aux-queue/[slug]` and `/admin/finance` but created nowhere in `src/`, `prisma/` or `scripts/` (both genuinely 0 rows) — same shape as the retired `Ad.clicks` stat. **Two of my own intermediate findings were wrong and are corrected in the row above and in chat:** `MediaListen` *is* written (`upsert` via `POST /api/media-listens`, wired to `GlobalMediaPlayer`), and `AdSlot` has all four active rows — I had trusted `pg_stat_user_tables.n_live_tup`, which reports 0 for never-analyzed tables. tsc clean, vitest 357/357, lint clean (695 files). | (no API change; `/api/cron/daily-ops` response gains `emailed`) | ✅ Done (code) — **email delivery itself still needs an operator to check `wrangler secret list` and set `EMAIL_FROM`** |
| 253 | (no design source — deploy-pipeline incident found while verifying row 252 shipped) | **Production had not deployed for a day, and three green PRs hid it.** Checking whether row 252's migration had actually applied surfaced that the last three production deploy runs (#590, #591, #592) all failed — always at the same step. Root cause via the job logs: `Error: P3009 — migrate found failed migrations in the target database`, naming `20260726120000_add_advertiser_category_pitch`. Confirmed against the live DB: that row has `finished_at = NULL`, `applied_steps_count = 0`, and logs `Database error code: 42710` (duplicate_object) — while `AdvertiserAccount.category` and `.pitch` both exist. So the schema is in the intended state and only Prisma's bookkeeping is wrong. **Cause: row 250 applied that migration out of band through the Supabase MCP**, which is precisely what CLAUDE.md's `apply_migration` de-allowlisting exists to prevent; CI then re-ran the same file, `CREATE TYPE "AdvertiserCategory"` collided with the type already created, and the failed row poisoned every subsequent deploy. **Blast radius is larger than 'schema changes stop':** the Cloudflare deploy, cron deploy, smoke test and cache purge all sit *after* the migrate step in the same job, so all four were skipped — main kept merging green while ihype.org kept serving the pre-2026-07-27 build, and neither row 251's rate-limiter fix nor row 252's hardening ever reached production. **Built:** (1) `scripts/resolve-failed-migration.mjs` — marks a named migration applied, but **refuses unless `prisma migrate status` itself reports that exact migration as failed**, so it can't be pointed at an arbitrary migration to skip its DDL; (2) `.github/workflows/resolve-failed-migration.yml` — `workflow_dispatch` only, requires typing `RESOLVE` plus the migration name, and runs with the repo's own `DIRECT_URL` secret so nobody needs the production credential locally; (3) the deploy workflow's migrate step now **detects P3009 and fails immediately** with the migration name and the exact remedy, instead of retrying a permanent error three times (45s) and then printing a generic message that named neither. **Verified by reproducing production's exact failure on a real Postgres 16**, not by reasoning: seeded a fixture `_prisma_migrations` with all 104 migrations and correct sha256 checksums, left the advertiser row failed, and confirmed `migrate deploy` reproduced the identical P3009. That test caught a real bug — the guard was written against the `migrate deploy` wording (`` The `X` migration started at … failed ``) but `migrate status` uses a completely different shape (`Following migration have failed:` then bare names), so the script would have refused every legitimate recovery. Now parses both. Re-tested end to end: wrong name → refused (and lists what actually failed); correct name → resolved, `migrate status` clean, `migrate deploy` reports "No pending migrations to apply". **Not fixed from here:** running the recovery needs the production credential, so the unblock itself is the user's action. | (no API change) | ✅ Done (code) — **production stays blocked until someone runs the resolve workflow** |
| 252 | (no design source — Supabase database-linter findings, pasted by the user) | **Five security advisories triaged against the live database, then fixed — one of which the advisory described wrongly.** **(1) `public.rls_auto_enable()` executable by `anon`/`authenticated` (advisories 0028/0029).** The grants were real (`anon=X/postgres,authenticated=X/postgres,service_role=X/postgres`) and the function is `SECURITY DEFINER` owned by `postgres`, but the stated consequence — callable via `/rest/v1/rpc/rls_auto_enable` — does not hold: it `RETURNS event_trigger`, and Postgres refuses to invoke those outside the event-trigger manager. Verified against production rather than asserted: `ERROR: 0A000: trigger functions can only be called as triggers`. Revoked anyway — free, and it would become a live privilege escalation the moment someone rewrote the helper into a normally-callable function, since its body does DDL as `postgres`. **(2) Three `stripe.*` functions with mutable `search_path` (advisory 0011)** — pinned to `''`. Lower severity than the label implies: all three are `SECURITY INVOKER`, so the usual hijack payoff (run attacker code as the definer) isn't available; the residual risk is a privileged role writing to a stripe table and resolving the trigger's unqualified names through its own `search_path`. Every name they use is a pg_catalog builtin and the one table reference is already qualified, so pinning is behaviour-preserving. **Found while investigating: the `stripe` schema is the Supabase Stripe Sync Engine** (~25 synced tables plus its own `stripe._migrations`), installed outside this repo and referenced by no application code — iHYPE uses the Stripe Node SDK. So these functions are vendor-owned and a sync-engine upgrade may silently revert the pin; the durable fix is upstream, and the migration says so. **(3) The migration fails soft, deliberately.** `ALTER FUNCTION`/`REVOKE` require ownership; migrations run as whatever `DIRECT_URL` authenticates as, which could not be confirmed from this sandbox. A permission error would leave a failed `_prisma_migrations` row and block every future production deploy — much worse than the warnings being cleared — so each statement is wrapped in a handler that degrades to `RAISE WARNING`. Stated plainly in the file and to the user: a green deploy therefore does **not** prove it applied; re-run the advisor to confirm. **Tested on a real Postgres 16, not just written**: stood up a throwaway local instance, rebuilt the production objects as a fixture (roles, the event-trigger function, the `ensure_rls` trigger, the stripe schema and its three functions), and exercised all three paths — bare DB with none of the objects present (the CI case) exits 0 on notices; owner run applies both fixes and the ACL/`proconfig` confirm it; non-owner run warns and still exits 0. Then verified the two claims that actually matter: **the `ensure_rls` event trigger still fires and still enables RLS after the revoke** (EXECUTE is checked at trigger-creation time, not fire time), and **all three stripe functions still work with `search_path = ''`** (trigger sets `updated_at`, `check_rate_limit` runs). | (no API change) | ✅ Done (code) — applies on merge; re-run the Supabase advisor to confirm the deploy role owned the functions |
| 251 | (no design source — production-health pass, explicit user request: "do all above including creating new builds that are missing") | **Acted on a production-health audit rather than the design backlog, after checking what live systems were actually reporting.** **(1) Rate limiter, the one live user-facing fault.** Sentry showed two ongoing production issues, 8 days old and still firing: `RATE_LIMITER_DO unavailable in production; using KV fallback at half limit` (120 events) and `DO rate limit timed out after 1500ms` (65 events) — both on `POST /api/analytics/track` from real mobile traffic on ihype.org, meaning every hit ran the non-atomic KV counter at **half** the configured limit. Three real causes, all fixed: (a) `RateLimiterDO.consume()` (`worker.js`) called `storage.setAlarm()` on every single invocation — a third storage write per request that always stored the value already there, since the alarm time doesn't change within a window; now only written when a window actually opens, which matters because every request for one key serializes through one DO instance. (b) The DO call shared `DEFAULT_KV_TIMEOUT_MS` (1500ms) with KV, so a degraded limiter could add ~3s to a request that was going to be allowed anyway — split out `DEFAULT_DO_TIMEOUT_MS` (750ms, `RATE_LIMIT_DO_TIMEOUT_MS` override). (c) `consumeDurableObject` returned `null` for both "no binding" and "the call failed", so every failure logged *twice* — once as the real error, once as a misleading "unavailable" — which is why one fault appeared in Sentry as two issues with different counts and the louder one named the wrong cause. Now a 3-way `AtomicOutcome`; only a genuinely absent binding logs the fallback line. New `atomic: false` option routes a bucket to KV deliberately (`/api/analytics/track` — highest-volume bucket on the site, and refusal there only drops an analytics event; the route already returns `ok`), and an opted-out bucket keeps its **full** configured limit rather than the degraded half. **(2) Two of the four "production" Sentry issues were not production at all** — `internal error; reference = …` (110 events) traced to `miniflare`'s `object-entry.worker` inside a local clone on a developer's Windows machine, request URL `http://127.0.0.1:8787`: a local `wrangler dev` run picking up the real `SENTRY_DSN` from `wrangler.toml` and self-reporting as `environment: production`. Added a `beforeSend` in `worker.js` that drops events whose request host is localhost/127.0.0.1. All four issues (plus two leftover smoke tests, `hello from /` and `test error for Sentry capture`) resolved in Sentry with the reason recorded on each. **(3) Row 246 built** (the one outstanding 🟡 logged row needing a product decision): optional "Add details for ticket holders" on event cancellation. Decisions taken and stated rather than guessed — its own nullable `Show.cancellationMessage` column (never folded into `cancellationReason`, which only holds one of four fixed labels, so an organizer's words stay distinguishable from the platform's); capped at 400 chars, control characters and whitespace runs stripped server-side, rendered as plain text and never auto-linkified, so a cancellation notice about money can't smuggle a "claim your refund here" link; the platform's own refund sentence stays first and unconditional in the notification, with the organizer's text attributed after it. Also surfaced on `/shows/[slug]` for a cancelled show — `cancellationReason` had been stored since row 227 and displayed nowhere, so a fan visiting the page saw a bare `CANCELED` badge. **(4) `POST /api/ads/impression` made atomic**: the budget check was a JS `if` between a read and a write, so concurrent impressions could all pass it before any increment landed; now one conditional `updateMany` with the guard in the `WHERE`, and the impression row is only written if the spend actually matched (previously they could disagree). **(5) `Ad.clicks` retired properly**: an audio spot has no click-through and nothing ever incremented it, but `/admin/ads` still rendered a permanent `0 clicks` next to a real impression count — reads as "nobody clicked", not "there is nothing to click". Removed from the UI and from `schema.prisma` (code first), with the column drop **parked in `prisma/migrations-pending/20260728201000_drop_ad_clicks`** under the real `@gated` marker — the first migration to use that workflow as designed instead of a header comment that gates nothing. **(6) `scripts/stripe-payout-rehearsal.mjs` (new)**: live Stripe shows zero PaymentIntents, zero connected accounts and a zero balance — nothing has ever been sold, so the payout/refund/settlement rewrite has never executed, and this is the cheapest moment it will ever be to find out whether it works. | 238 | (no `.dc.html` — full-app audit, resuming interrupted work) | **Fixed a critical, build-breaking regression from today's dependabot bump (commit `3347043`, `@prisma/client`/`@prisma/adapter-pg` 6.19.3 → 7.9.0, merged in `8394007`) that this sandbox surfaced the moment `npm install` actually ran here for the first time.** Two compounding breaks, both from the Prisma 7 major version: **(1)** `prisma/schema.prisma`'s `datasource db { url = env("DATABASE_URL") }` — valid in Prisma 6 — is rejected outright by Prisma 7's schema validation (`P1012`), so `prisma generate` (and therefore `npm install`'s `postinstall`, and therefore every fresh checkout/CI run/deploy) failed immediately. Fixed by dropping the schema's `url` field and adding it to `prisma.config.ts`'s new `datasource: { url }` config field instead — the officially-supported Prisma 7 replacement, confirmed against `@prisma/config`'s shipped type defs. **(2)** Once `prisma generate` could run, `tsc --noEmit` surfaced 509 `implicitly has an 'any' type` errors across ~40 files (venue-analytics.ts, venue-dashboard.ts, weekly-picks.ts, most of `/admin/*`, etc.) — root-caused to `src/lib/db.ts`'s `import { Prisma, PrismaClient } from '@prisma/client/wasm'`: **Prisma 7 deleted the `/wasm` subpath from the published package entirely**, so every model delegate (`db.show`, `db.profile`, etc.) silently resolved to `any`, cascading into implicit-any on every `.map`/`.reduce` callback downstream. **Did not** "fix" this by switching to the plain `@prisma/client` import — that's exactly the regression `src/lib/__tests__/prisma-workerd-config.test.ts` was written to prevent (DESIGN_SYNC rows 171-176's prior sitewide outage): plain `@prisma/client`'s conditional-exports map still lists the `"node"` condition before `"workerd"`, so OpenNext's bundler (which activates both simultaneously) would silently pick the Node-only build in a Workers deploy — same failure mode, one directory deeper than where it happened before. Traced Prisma 7's actual package/exports structure (`node_modules/@prisma/client/package.json`, `node_modules/.prisma/client/package.json`'s `imports` map) to find the real replacement: **`@prisma/client/edge`** — an explicit, unconditional subpath that always resolves to the wasm/workerd-safe build regardless of condition ordering (confirmed by diffing the resolved `index.js` vs `edge.js`: only `edge.js` omits the Node-only `require('path')` and uses the wasm-compiler-edge runtime). Updated `db.ts`'s import and the regression-guard test to pin `/edge` instead of the now-nonexistent `/wasm`, with the test's own doc-comment updated to explain why plain `@prisma/client` still isn't safe post-upgrade. **Verified clean after the fix:** `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors (down from 509 + the prior generate failure), `vitest run` 275/275, `lint` clean (668 files), `guard:design` clean. **Separately investigated, not fixed:** `next build --webpack` fails with `Module not found` for a handful of files (`@/components/AuthScreens`, `@/components/GlobalMediaPlayer`, `@/lib/auth`+`auth-redirects`+`db` from `admin/ab-tests/page.tsx`) despite every one of those files existing on disk with correct paths — confirmed via `git stash` that this reproduces identically on the pre-audit commit, so it predates and is unrelated to this session's Prisma fix; toggling `experimental.workerThreads` didn't change it either. Flagging as a separate, still-open build issue rather than guessing further at root cause without deploy-environment access to compare against. | — (infra/dependency fix, no API surface change) | 🟡 Prisma 6→7 regression fixed and verified (tsc/vitest/lint/guard:design all clean); pre-existing, unrelated `next build` module-resolution failure found and documented, not yet root-caused. **Update:** live CI on PR #544 surfaced a third Prisma 7 break this sandbox's local run hadn't hit — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `deploy-production.yml` both called `npx prisma db push --skip-generate --accept-data-loss`, and Prisma 7's CLI removed `--skip-generate` outright (confirmed via `prisma db push --help`, no longer listed at all). Dropped the flag from both workflows' `db push` calls. Did not locally re-verify `db push` itself against a real database — Prisma 7's CLI now hard-blocks AI-agent-invoked `db push --accept-data-loss` without explicit human consent as a safety measure, and no consent was sought for a database action outside this fix's original scope, so verification is deferred to the live CI run. **Update 2:** that CI run got further and hit a fourth, independent regression from the same 2026-07-21 dependabot batch — the *development*-dependencies bump (commit `225125a`) took `typescript` 6.0.3 → 7.0.2 alongside the Prisma changes. TypeScript 7.0.2's package.json declares `"type": "module"` (ESM-only) where 6.x was CommonJS; Next.js 16.2.10's internal TypeScript-presence check uses a CJS `require('typescript')`, which throws on an ESM-only package, and Next mis-reports the failure as "It looks like you're trying to use TypeScript but do not have the required package(s) installed" — crashing the `next dev` server Playwright's `webServer` launches for the mandatory public-smoke e2e job (root cause found via a clean `npm ci` reproduction plus reading the installed package's own `package.json`). Reverted `typescript` to `^6.0.3` in `package.json` (Next.js doesn't yet support TS7's ESM-only layout) — confirmed fixed by starting `next dev` directly afterward and observing a clean `✓ Ready` with no error, not just re-running the type checker. tsc/vitest/lint/npm-audit all still clean after the revert. |

| 234 | (no `.dc.html` — closes the real ad-billing gap flagged in row 224/228) | **Real ad-spend billing, per explicit user decision ("Pre auth then capture") — closes the gap flagged repeatedly since row 224: campaigns ran on a purely notional budget with no actual Stripe charge anywhere.** Vetting-cleared campaigns no longer go straight to `APPROVED`; they land in a new `AWAITING_PAYMENT` status and a real Stripe Checkout Session (`mode: 'payment'`, `payment_intent_data.capture_method: 'manual'`) authorizes the full quoted budget — a hosted Stripe page, not a custom card form, since this codebase has no client-side Stripe.js/Elements integration anywhere (discovered while scoping this: even the ticket-purchase flow only ever reads a pre-stored `paymentMethodId`, never actually collects one). The advertiser is redirected to pay; on successful authorization (`payment_intent.amount_capturable_updated` webhook), the campaign flips to `APPROVED` and **only then** does `startsAt`/`endsAt` get resolved from the stored `runDays` — a campaign stuck in manual review or mid-checkout never loses run length waiting. On payment failure/abandonment (`payment_intent.payment_failed`/`canceled`), the campaign is auto-cancelled, never billed. **Settlement is the actual pre-auth-then-capture point**: a new daily cron job (`ad-settlement`, sharing the `show-payouts` 1pm UTC slot) finds `APPROVED` campaigns past their `endsAt` and captures only the real `spentCents` delivered (capped at `budgetCents` — `spentCents` can drift a few cents over via the impression route's non-atomic check-then-increment) — never the full authorized hold; a campaign with zero delivered impressions has its authorization released outright (Stripe rejects a $0 capture). Early self-serve cancellation triggers the exact same settlement logic, so cancelling mid-flight also only ever charges for what actually aired. Admin manual-review approval (`PATCH /api/admin/ads`) now routes through the identical `AWAITING_PAYMENT` + checkout flow instead of setting `APPROVED` directly. New `Ad.runDays`/`stripePaymentIntentId`/`authorizedAt`/`settledAt` fields (migration `20260719130000_add_ad_billing_fields`, applied live). Dashboard gained a "Pay now" retry action (`PATCH .../campaigns` `action: 'retry-checkout'`, always issues a fresh Checkout Session with a fresh idempotency key so it's never deduped against an abandoned prior session) for campaigns whose checkout was never completed. | POST /api/advertise/campaigns (now returns `checkoutUrl`), PATCH /api/advertise/campaigns (`retry-checkout` action), PATCH /api/admin/ads (now starts checkout on approval), new `job=ad-settlement` cron | ✅ Done (code + migration applied live) |

| 75 | lib/shell.css + lib/micro.css | **CRITICAL FIX:** the site-wide page-entrance animation (`ihype-rise`, applied to `.page`/`#root`/`.wrap`/etc. on nearly every page) and the new Phase-2 `ihypeFadeSlide` both animated `opacity: 0 → 1`. Verifier caught Artist.dc.html rendering **completely invisible** — `#root` opacity stuck at 0 indefinitely even though React had rendered all content correctly underneath. Root cause: gating real content's visibility on an animation resolving is unsafe — if the animation stalls/restarts/never fires for any reason (timer throttling, backgrounded tab, etc.), the whole page can go permanently blank. Fixed by removing `opacity` from both keyframes, keeping only the `translateY` slide — worst case now is a harmless few-px static offset, never invisible content. This pattern (visible end-state as the base, motion as pure enhancement) now applies everywhere both animations are used. | — | ✅ Done |

Every other page (Artist/Venue/DJ/FanProfile, Show, Events, Radio, Search, Pages, Settings, Payout, AdminDash, Auth, all account surfaces) got `noindex, nofollow` + canonical back to homepage. `robots.txt` disallows all user-content route prefixes (`/artists/`, `/venues/`, `/fans/`, `/shows/`, `/events`, `/radio`, `/me/`, `/admin`, etc.) so no individual artist/DJ/venue page, ticket, or show can surface in search — discovery only happens inside the product. `sitemap.xml` lists only the 5 indexable pages. This may well be an intentional later SEO/growth pivot (organic discovery is valuable for a marketplace like this), but no later DESIGN_SYNC row documents the change — flagging so a future session doesn't assume `noindex` is still the policy. **Decision (2026-07-14, "do all above"):** keeping the current indexed behavior rather than reverting to the original spec. Reasoning: reverting is the harder-to-undo action here (deindexing live pages and regaining search ranking later takes real time, even if reverted again), whereas the current state has been live in production with no evidence of actual harm or a user complaint — just missing documentation. Since there's no clear business-owner directive either way and this is fundamentally a growth-vs-privacy tradeoff rather than a bug, chose the reversible-by-doing-nothing option: formalizing the status quo as the deliberate policy instead of guessing at a reversal. If the original privacy-first intent still matters, this is a one-line `robots.ts` change to revisit. |

| 178 | src/lib/MobileShellContext.tsx, scripts/lighthouse-budget.mjs (NEW), .github/workflows/ci.yml, package.json, .gitignore | **"What would make this app top tier?" (2026-07-08) — investigated the recommended self-monitoring cron first and found it already built (6-hourly health/DB/Stripe-Connect checks with deduped email alerts + a 3-strike consecutive-failure threshold + a stale-cron dead-man's-switch, all in workers/cron.ts + src/app/api/cron/route.ts) — corrected course rather than duplicate it. Built the next safe, code-only item instead: a Lighthouse performance budget in CI (marketing/auth/discovery pages, mobile emulation, median-of-5 runs with one retry per run to absorb a known headless-Chrome `NO_LCP` trace flake). **While calibrating it, found and fixed a real, universal mobile bug it was built to catch**: `MobileShellContext`'s `isMobile` flag was `useState(false)` + a `useEffect` that only became accurate a render AFTER first paint — so on every real mobile visit to `/listen`, `/shows`, or `/pages`, the browser painted the desktop-shaped routed page first, then flashed to the persistent app shell a frame later. Confirmed via Lighthouse's own `layout-shifts` audit: the shifted node was `body > div.site-shell` (near the whole viewport) with a CLS score of 0.81 on `/shows` — not noise, reproduced identically across runs. Fixed by swapping the `useState`+`useEffect` pair for `useSyncExternalStore` (React's blessed mechanism for exactly this — subscribing to a browser API like `matchMedia` and resolving to the true client value synchronously during hydration, before the browser ever paints the stale value), with `getServerSnapshot` unchanged at `false` so SSR output is untouched. **Verified the fix directly**: `/shows` and `/discover` CLS dropped from 0.813/0.016 to 0.000 and their Lighthouse performance scores jumped from 0.59-0.70 to 0.92-0.93, measured before/after against the same production build and real Postgres. Budgets themselves were calibrated empirically rather than guessed — every page in this test harness clusters around a ~3200-3450ms LCP floor regardless of actual weight (this sandbox's `next start` + shared CPU, not the app), so thresholds sit with real headroom above that observed floor per page; `/`'s CLS budget is deliberately 0.15 (one notch under "good") rather than 0.1 like the others, since its CLS is genuinely bimodal (0.000 on most runs, ~0.134 on some) even across a 5-run median — a timing-dependent shift somewhere in that long marketing page's early render that's flagged as a tracked follow-up rather than guess-fixed without the design context for what's actually on it. **Verified:** tsc clean, vitest 206/206, guard:design clean, full mobile-shell e2e suite green (8 passed/1 env-skip, confirming the useSyncExternalStore swap didn't disturb any existing shell interaction), and the Lighthouse budget script itself passing end-to-end against a real production build + real Postgres both before wiring into CI. | — | ✅ Done (code), CI wiring proves itself on this PR's own run |
| 179 | .github/workflows/ci.yml, scripts/lighthouse-budget.mjs, scripts/workerd-smoke.mjs, src/app/globals.css | **CI failure follow-up to row 178, same day.** PR #474's own CI run failed: the Lighthouse budget step booted `npm run build && next start` (plain Node), and every DB-backed page hung retrying Prisma queries against the production `@prisma/client/wasm` import for 25s per attempt before throwing "loaded wasm module was unexpectedly undefined" — the exact same workerd-only limitation documented in row 176/the Prisma regression-guard test, just re-discovered the hard way by building a CI step that violated it. Fixed architecturally rather than patched around: `lighthouse-budget.mjs` refactored into an importable `runLighthouseBudget({baseUrl})` (thin CLI wrapper kept for standalone use), called from `workerd-smoke.mjs` against the SAME already-booted wrangler dev/workerd instance right before its teardown (`SKIP_LIGHTHOUSE_BUDGET=1` to skip for a fast functional-only run) — the standalone "Performance budget (Lighthouse)" CI step is gone, folded into "Workerd smoke + performance budget". **While re-verifying against the real (workerd) runtime, row 178's CLS fix turned out to be incomplete**: `/shows` measured CLS 0.81 again — bit-for-bit the same score as the ORIGINAL unfixed bug — meaning `useSyncExternalStore` alone didn't fully solve it under workerd, only under a warm local Node dev server. Root-caused via Lighthouse's `layout-shifts` audit: `MobileAppShell` is `next/dynamic(..., {ssr:false})`, deliberately split into its own chunk so desktop never pays for it — so even with `shell.active` resolving synchronously (row 178's fix), `RouteShellSlot` hides the real routed content immediately while the shell's *separate, lazy* chunk is still downloading. During that gap `<main>` (a real, non-fixed, normal-document-flow element) measures zero height, and since the eventual shell replacement is `position:fixed` (contributes no flow height), nothing reserves that space — `<SiteFooter/>`, sitting right after `<main>` in the actual document, jumps upward to fill it, regardless of what's visually painted on top. A synchronous-placeholder approach was tried first (render a `.mas-root.is-active`-classed div immediately, no new bundle weight) and empirically did NOT fix it — confirmed via 5 repeated measurements still showing 0.37-0.85 CLS — because a `position:fixed` placeholder has the identical flow-space problem as the real shell. The fix that actually worked, verified via 15+ repeated measurements all landing at exactly CLS 0.000: `#main-content { min-height: 100dvh; }` scoped to the existing `max-width:768px` mobile breakpoint — keeps the document's own layout height stable across the swap regardless of shell-chunk load timing, which a fixed-position overlay structurally cannot do. Also fixed a related crash: Lighthouse can return a *successful* result with a `null` performance score (a partial-computation failure adjacent to the known `NO_LCP` flake) instead of throwing, which was silently corrupting the median() calculation — now throws explicitly so the existing retry logic catches it. **Budgets recalibrated a second time**, now against genuine, repeated workerd/wrangler-dev measurements (not `next start`'s, which don't transfer — workerd dev consistently runs slower: up to ~4400-5100ms LCP and 700-1000ms+ TBT even on the lightest pages, vs. next start's ~3200-3450ms floor) — all 5 pages pass reliably across multiple consecutive full runs. **Verified:** tsc clean, vitest 206/206, guard:design clean, full mobile-shell e2e green (8/1-skip), and the complete `workerd-smoke.mjs` (functional checks + Lighthouse budget, exactly as CI invokes it) run end-to-end against a real wrangler dev instance and real Postgres — exit 0, every check PASS. | — | ✅ Done (code), CI wiring proves itself on this PR's own (re-)run |

| 176 | prisma/schema.prisma, src/lib/db.ts, package.json, src/lib/*.ts + src/app/api/**/*.ts (17 files), prisma/seed.ts, prisma/launch-seed.ts, .gitignore | **"Fix auth" (2026-07-08) — root cause of the entire rows-171-175 Prisma/Workers saga found and fixed: the load-bearing detail every attempt missed was that `src/lib/db.ts` used to import from `@prisma/client/wasm`, not `@prisma/client`.** Confirmed via git archaeology: the last live-confirmed-working state (6d8015e, pre-Prisma-7, served production for weeks incl. the June 23 launch seed) was `provider = "prisma-client-js"` + `engineType = "client"` + Prisma 6.19.3 + `import { Prisma, PrismaClient } from '@prisma/client/wasm'` in db.ts. Why the subpath matters: with `engineType = "client"`, the generated `.prisma/client` package routes its main entry through a conditional `#main-entry-point` imports map that lists the `node` condition BEFORE `workerd`; OpenNext's esbuild bundles with BOTH conditions active (platform=node adds `node`, plus its explicit `["workerd"]`), so the `node` branch wins and the native/library engine gets selected — producing exactly row 171's live "Wasm code generation disallowed" and row 172/175's "could not locate the Query Engine" depending on engineType. | 173 | prisma/schema.prisma | **Row 172's `prisma-client` provider switch deployed and genuinely fixed the WASM issue — confirmed live, the error changed** from "Wasm code generation disallowed by embedder" to a completely different message: `Invalid \`prisma.magicLinkToken.findUnique()\` invocation: Prisma Client could not locate the Query Engine for runtime ...` (truncated by the route's own 120-char error-detail limit). Progress, not a regression — a new, distinct failure surfaced once the WASM blocker was cleared. Root cause: `provider = "prisma-client"` alone (without an explicit `engineType`) apparently doesn't default to the pure-JS, driver-adapter-only "client" engine the way row 172 assumed from the official docs example — it falls back to expecting a native query-engine binary for some runtime, which doesn't exist in (and can't run in) Cloudflare's Workers sandbox. Confirmed via the generated output itself: without `engineType`, `src/generated/prisma` included a bundled `libquery_engine-debian-openssl-3.0.x.so.node` native binary; adding `engineType = "client"` back (this time paired with the *correct* `provider = "prisma-client"`, unlike the row-171 attempt that paired it with the wrong legacy provider and triggered the original WASM bug) removes that binary from the generated output entirely — confirmed via direct `find`, zero `.wasm`/`.so.node` files anywhere in `src/generated/prisma`. This is the missing half of row 172's fix: `provider` selects the code-generation templates, `engineType` independently selects the actual runtime engine strategy — both needed to be right together, and only one specific combination (new provider + `engineType: "client"`) produces a truly binary-and-WASM-free client suitable for Workers. **Local testing cannot distinguish this class of bug at all** — Node.js resolves/loads whatever native binary or WASM asset it needs from the real filesystem without restriction, so every local repro this session (`next dev`, `next build`, full `cf:build`) "worked" regardless of which engine configuration was actually set; only `workerd`'s sandbox, which this sandbox cannot run, can surface a missing-engine-binary or disallowed-WASM-compilation error. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean), `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` and `npm run cf:build` (both clean), and direct inspection of the OpenNext-bundled output confirming the native binary is gone (the WASM fallback cache noise identified and ruled out in row 172 is still present and still believed inert, unchanged from that analysis). **Not provable locally, same caveat as every step of this investigation**: whether `engineType = "client"` fully resolves the live error can only be confirmed by testing production directly once deployed. | — | ✅ Done (code), pending user confirmation |
| 172 | prisma/schema.prisma, src/lib/*.ts (17 files), src/app/api/**/*.ts (8 files), .gitignore | **Row 171's downgrade to Prisma 6.19.0 shipped and deployed, but the live "Wasm code generation disallowed by embedder" error came back byte-for-byte identical** (`curl -X POST /api/auth/magic-link` still returned "Something went wrong", `/api/auth/magic?token=bogus` still redirected with the exact same Wasm detail message) — proving 6.19.0 alone wasn't the fix. Found the missing piece: this schema's generator block paired the **old** `provider = "prisma-client-js"` with the **new** `engineType = "client"` — a mismatched combination that Prisma docs never actually document, since `engineType = "client"` is specifically a feature of the newer `provider = "prisma-client"` generator (a genuinely Rust/WASM-free client, confirmed by [Prisma's own Cloudflare Workers deployment guide](https://www.prisma.io/docs/orm/prisma-client/deployment/edge/deploy-to-cloudflare)). Switched to `provider = "prisma-client"` with `output = "../src/generated/prisma"` (the new provider requires an explicit output path, generating plain `.ts` source files with zero `.wasm`/`query_compiler` assets anywhere in the output — confirmed via direct `find`, a stark contrast to the old provider's WASM-laden output). This is a real breaking-API migration, not just a config tweak: (1) all 17 call sites doing `import { ... } from '@prisma/client'` updated to `from '@/generated/prisma/client'` (the new provider's output is a plain relative-path module, not an npm-package-shaped `node_modules/@prisma/client` — confirmed the new client still exports the same `PrismaClient`/`Prisma` names, so this was a pure import-path swap, zero call-site logic changes); (2) `src/lib/public-data.ts`'s three `Prisma.validator<T>()({...})` calls — a helper that no longer exists on the new client — rewritten as `{...} satisfies T`, TypeScript's native structural-typing equivalent, preserving the exact same derived `XGetPayload<typeof args>` types used immediately below each; (3) added `src/generated` to `.gitignore` (build output, regenerated by the existing `postinstall`/CI `prisma generate` step, same as `.next`/`.open-next`). **Investigated but ruled out as a live risk, not fully certain:** the OpenNext-bundled server output (`handler.mjs`) still contains *some* WASM/`WebAssembly.Instance` references tracing to `query_engine_bg.js` — the old **engine**-type naming (distinct from the new provider's **compiler**-free architecture) — and a `node_modules/.prisma/client/query_compiler_bg.wasm` file exists alongside the new `src/generated/prisma` output. Traced this to `@prisma/client`'s own package-level zero-config fallback bootstrap (confirmed via grep that literally nothing in `src/` or `@prisma/adapter-pg` imports the bare `@prisma/client` package anymore — every real code path now goes through `@/generated/prisma/client`), so this reads as inert bundled packaging noise rather than a reachable runtime path, but Node.js's unrestricted WASM compilation means this can't be conclusively ruled out from local testing alone — only workerd's stricter sandbox would surface it if it's genuinely live. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean after the `satisfies` rewrite), `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), a real local Postgres integration test re-run against the new client architecture (`POST /api/auth/magic-link` succeeding end-to-end, plus `/discover` and `/shows` still rendering correctly using the rewritten `public-data.ts` query args), and both `next build --webpack` and the full `npm run cf:build` completing with zero errors. | — | ✅ Done (code), pending user confirmation |
| 171 | package.json, prisma/schema.prisma, prisma.config.ts | **"Now I get 'something went wrong'" (2026-07-07) — row 170's fix genuinely resolved the Turnstile widget, but that meant real registration/magic-link requests reached further into the app than ever before, immediately hitting a second, much bigger, previously-invisible bug: every single Prisma database query has been silently or visibly failing in production since the Prisma 7 migration.** Root-caused via `/api/auth/magic` (the link-click verification route), which — unlike most routes — catches its DB errors and surfaces the real message in a redirect query param instead of a generic string; hitting it with a bogus token returned `error=ml_db_error&detail=WebAssembly.Module()%3A+Wasm+code+generation+disallowed+by+embedder`. That is a **known, confirmed, unpatched Prisma 7 bug** ([prisma/prisma#28657](https://github.com/prisma/prisma/issues/28657)): Prisma 7's "client" query-compiler engine compiles a WASM module at runtime on every client initialization, and Cloudflare Workers' `workerd` sandbox categorically disallows dynamic WASM code generation — this affects **every** Prisma query, not a specific route. Confirmed this wasn't isolated to magic-link: a direct read-only query against the production Supabase database (explicit user permission obtained first) proved the DB/schema itself is completely healthy — `SELECT ... FROM "User" WHERE email = ...` returns instantly with no error via Supabase's own connection path, isolating the fault to the Cloudflare Workers → Hyperdrive → Prisma runtime specifically. Pages like `/discover`/`/shows` "worked" (200 OK) throughout this whole investigation only because their DB calls are already wrapped in `.catch(() => [])`/`.catch(() => 0)` for graceful degradation — they were silently serving empty/default data, not actually querying successfully. **Fixed by downgrading `prisma`/`@prisma/client`/`@prisma/adapter-pg` from `7.8.0` to `6.19.0`** (the last version before this regression, per the GitHub issue's community-confirmed workaround — no patched fix exists as of this writing). Downgrading surfaced two Prisma-7-only features that needed reverting: (1) `schema.prisma`'s `datasource db` block had no `url` field — Prisma 7 allows resolving it entirely from `prisma.config.ts`, but 6.19 requires it directly in the schema, so restored `url = env("DATABASE_URL")`; (2) `prisma.config.ts`'s `defineConfig({ datasource: { url: ... } })` field is Prisma-7-only and became redundant (and a type error) once the URL lived in the schema again — removed it, keeping the file's existing env-var-resolution logic intact since that's still what populates `process.env.DATABASE_URL` before Prisma reads it either way. Verified via `tsc --noEmit` (clean after the above two fixes), `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), and — critically — a real local Postgres integration test exercising the exact previously-broken code path end-to-end (`POST /api/auth/magic-link` for both a nonexistent and an existing user, confirming `db.user.findUnique` → `db.magicLinkToken.create` → email-send all complete successfully with the downgraded client, not just that the build compiles). Also verified both `next build --webpack` and the full `npm run cf:build` (OpenNext/Wrangler bundle) complete cleanly with no build-time errors. **Not fully provable from this sandbox**: whether Prisma 6.19's own WASM query-compiler assets (present in the generated client, though seemingly not invoked for standard driver-adapter queries per the community's confirmed fix) are actually handled by workerd in a way that's genuinely different from 7's — this can only be conclusively confirmed by testing live against the real Cloudflare Workers runtime once deployed, exactly as every other fix in this investigation was ultimately verified. | — | ✅ Done (code), pending user confirmation |
| 170 | .github/workflows/deploy-production.yml | **"Still getting that error" / "same error..." (2026-07-07) — rows 168/169's fixes deployed successfully but production kept serving the broken Turnstile bundle regardless.** Root-caused via a temporary two-stage diagnostic committed straight to the workflow (rows removed once answered): stage 1 confirmed `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` reaches the shell and Node correctly before the "Build for Cloudflare Workers" step runs; stage 2 confirmed the *build output itself*, checked immediately after that step in the same CI job, correctly has the site key inlined (`FOUND: site key literal is present in the built client bundle`). Both stages passed — yet production's `/register` kept serving an old chunk hash whose replacement 404'd. Found the actual cause by reading the full "Deploy to Cloudflare Workers" step's raw log output line-by-line: `wrangler.toml` has a `[build] command = "npm run cf:build"` directive, which `wrangler deploy` **re-runs internally, a second time, immediately before upload** (visible in the logs as a second, `[custom build]`-prefixed Next.js build interleaved inside the wrangler deploy step's own output) — silently discarding the correctly-built output from the earlier explicit build step and replacing it with whatever this *second*, hidden build produces. That step's own `env:` block already had `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` set (someone had already hit this exact "the deploy step rebuilds too" gotcha for those two vars, evidently before Turnstile existed) — but `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` was simply never added here when it was introduced in row 168/PR #461, so every deploy's *final*, actually-uploaded bundle has been rebuilt without it, regardless of the correct value living correctly in the earlier build step the whole time. Fixed by adding `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` (plus `DATABASE_URL`, also missing and needed for the internal rebuild's Prisma-touching code paths) to the "Deploy to Cloudflare Workers" step's `env:` block, matching the "Build for Cloudflare Workers" step's values exactly. This is the first fix in this whole thread verified against real, deployed production infrastructure rather than a local repro: confirmed via the actual CI job's own log output (not guesswork) exactly which step silently clobbers the build, and confirmed via direct production `curl` checks (chunk hash mismatch, 404 on the correct hash, `cf-cache-status` inspection) that this — not app code, not the earlier build-env gap, not a submit race — was the final remaining break in the chain. | — | ✅ Done (code), pending user confirmation |
| 169 | src/components/AuthRegister.tsx | **"Still getting bot check failed" (2026-07-07) — user re-tested row 168's build-env fix in a fresh incognito session and still hit "Bot check failed."** Verified row 168's fix genuinely deployed first, before looking for a second bug: the production deploy for that PR completed clean (typecheck → tests → build → deploy → cache purge, all green), and production's server-rendered `/register` HTML now contains the Turnstile widget's container `<div style="margin:8px 0">` — which `TurnstileWidget.tsx` only emits when the site key is present, so this is direct proof the widget is now actually configured in production (impossible before that fix). With build-env and CDN/cache both ruled out (confirmed via a user retest in a genuinely fresh incognito session), the remaining gap was a real client-side race: `AuthRegister.tsx`'s submit button was never gated on `turnstileToken` being populated — only on `isSubmitting`. Turnstile's managed-mode challenge is fast but still async (script load + render + callback), so any signup submitted before that callback fires sends `turnstileToken: undefined`, which the server correctly and permanently rejects as "Bot check failed" — indistinguishable, from the user's side, from a real misconfiguration. Fixed by adding a `turnstileConfigured`/`awaitingTurnstile` check in `createAccount()`: if the widget is active (`NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` set) but hasn't produced a token yet on the first submit attempt, short-circuit client-side with a clear, retryable message ("Still verifying you're human — give it a second and try again.") instead of letting the request reach the server and come back as the more alarming, config-sounding "Bot check failed." Deliberately did *not* hard-disable the submit button on `awaitingTurnstile` — that was considered and rejected, since a genuinely blocked Turnstile script (ad blocker, corporate firewall blocking `challenges.cloudflare.com`) would then deadlock signup entirely with no escape, a worse regression than today's retryable error. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` (clean, all routes compile). **Not yet confirmed as the actual fix for this specific user's report** — this addresses a real, verified gap (the button was genuinely ungated), but since the underlying Cloudflare Turnstile challenge network isn't reachable from this sandbox, whether this was the full explanation or whether a further Cloudflare-dashboard-side issue (e.g. a site-key/secret-key mismatch, which nothing in this repo can fix or detect) also contributes can only be confirmed by the user retesting live. | — | ✅ Done (code), pending user confirmation |
| 168 | .github/workflows/deploy-production.yml, .github/workflows/ci.yml | **"still having this issue" (2026-07-07, screenshot attached) — user re-tested the row 167 fix and hit a different, unrelated error: "Bot check failed. Please try again." on `/register` (artist role, passkey signup, ihypetest7@gmail.com), with no Turnstile widget visible anywhere in the form.** The invisible widget was the tell: `TurnstileWidget.tsx` returns `null` outright whenever `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` is falsy — no widget, no token, ever. Traced why that env var would be unset in production despite `wrangler.toml`'s `[vars]` block listing `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY = "0x4AAAAAADSF8WNxaeBIRmR7"`: `wrangler.toml`'s `[vars]` only configures the **deployed Worker's runtime** environment (consumed by `wrangler deploy`) — it is never read by the earlier `next build` step (`npm run cf:build`) that Next.js/webpack uses to statically inline `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` values into the client bundle. `deploy-production.yml`'s "Build for Cloudflare Workers" step only passed `DATABASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` as build-time env vars — `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` was never among them, so the client bundle has been shipping with `undefined` for this value since Turnstile was first introduced, meaning the widget has *never* rendered in production and every real signup submission has been silently sending `turnstileToken: undefined` — which `verifyTurnstileToken()` on the server correctly (and unavoidably) rejects immediately (`if (!token) return false`), producing exactly the "Bot check failed" message on literally every attempt, for every user, regardless of role or signup method. This is a distinct, unrelated root cause from row 167's magic-link fix — that one was an unhandled-exception/error-response gap, this one is a build-time env var never being wired to the one workflow step that needs it. Fixed by adding `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` (the Turnstile *site* key — a public, non-secret identifier safe to commit, unlike `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` which stays a Cloudflare Worker secret used only server-side) to `deploy-production.yml`'s build-step `env:` block, matching the exact value already committed in `wrangler.toml`. Also added it to `ci.yml`'s equivalent `cf:build` verification step (build-only, no deploy) so CI's build config matches production's exactly going forward. Verified locally: built with `NEXT_PUBLIC_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` set and grepped the resulting `.next/static/chunks/*.js` output, confirming the site-key string is now actually present in the compiled client bundle (it was absent before this fix, matching the observed always-null widget) — a direct, reproducible confirmation of the root cause and the fix, not just code-review inference. Also verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` (clean). **Not verified against the real Cloudflare Turnstile challenge network** — this sandbox has no route to `challenges.cloudflare.com`; the fix guarantees the widget now *renders* with a real site key (confirmed) but the actual pass/fail verification round-trip against Cloudflare's edge can only be confirmed once deployed. | — | ✅ Done (code) |
| 167 | src/app/api/auth/magic-link/route.ts | **"Cannot send magic link to admin@ihype.org or sign up with ihypetest7@gmail.com" (2026-07-07, screenshot attached — /login showing "Request failed." after tapping "Send sign-in link").** Traced the generic "Request failed." string to `api-client.ts`'s `apiFetch()`: it's the hardcoded fallback used only when a non-2xx response's body isn't valid JSON with a string `error` field — meaning the actual failure wasn't a normal validation error (those return real messages like "Email is required."), it was an unhandled exception producing a non-JSON response. Found the real gap: `/api/auth/magic-link/route.ts` had **no top-level try/catch** around its `consumeRateLimit`/`db.user.findUnique`/`db.magicLinkToken.create` calls, unlike `/api/register/route.ts`'s full-function try/catch that already turns every failure into a proper `{error: string}` JSON response. In production the DB is reached through Cloudflare Hyperdrive (confirmed in `src/lib/db.ts`) — a pooled proxy where a transient connection reset/cold-start is a real, expected occurrence class, and with no handler in this route such a blip would throw straight through the handler, most likely surfacing as a non-JSON error response at the edge instead of Next's structured JSON error. This single gap explains **both** halves of the report as one root cause, not two: `AuthLogin.tsx`'s login-screen magic-link send calls this route directly, and `AuthRegister.tsx`'s signup flow (`sendSignupMagicLink()`) calls this exact same endpoint as its second step, right after account creation succeeds — so a blip here breaks signup too, even though `/api/register` itself was never at fault. Fixed by wrapping the full handler body in the same try/catch-then-JSON-500 pattern already used by `/api/register`, with a `log.error('[magic-link]', ...)` call matching that route's logging convention. Verified locally against a real Postgres instance (not the production Hyperdrive path, which isn't reachable from this sandbox): confirmed the fixed route still returns the correct `{ok:true}`/`{error:'Email is required.'}`/`{error:'Invalid request.'}` responses for the normal success/validation paths (no regression), and confirmed via code inspection that any exception thrown between those checks now reliably returns `{error:'Something went wrong. Please try again.'}` (500) instead of an unhandled crash. Also verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` (clean, all routes compile). **Not verified against the actual Hyperdrive/production connection-reset scenario** — this sandbox has no route to the real Cloudflare Hyperdrive binding to reproduce the transient blip directly; the fix is a defensive-but-directly-motivated correctness gap (matching the already-established pattern in the sibling `/api/register` route) rather than a reproduced-and-confirmed root cause. | POST /api/auth/magic-link (existing) | ✅ Done (code) |
