| 164 | TurnstileWidget.tsx, AuthRegister.tsx | **"Error when trying to setup test artist account" — "Bot check failed. Please try again." on registration (2026-07-07, screenshot attached).** Also chased down (but could not reproduce) a separate prior report of "Known login says passkey verification failed" on `/login` — built a full local repro rig for that first (real local Postgres in this sandbox, `prisma db push` against it, then drove the actual `/register`→`/login` pages in a real browser via Chrome DevTools' WebAuthn virtual-authenticator API — not a mock, an actual credential-create/get ceremony against the live Prisma 7 + `@prisma/adapter-pg` stack this session migrated to). Ruled out, with direct evidence: Prisma 7's `Bytes`-field change (`Buffer`→`Uint8Array`) corrupting stored public keys (wrote a byte-for-byte round-trip test through the real client — bytes matched exactly); a full register→sign-out→sign-in-with-passkey cycle (succeeded); three repeated sign-out/sign-in rounds to catch counter/replay-tracking bugs the way real synced Apple/Google passkeys — which freeze `signCount` at 0 — are known to break naive implementations (all three succeeded, `@simplewebauthn/server`'s counter check already handles the frozen-zero case correctly). Could not reproduce that one and reported back asking for repro details instead of shipping a speculative fix. **This session's next message turned out to be the real, adjacent bug**, caught while setting up a test account the same way: registration failing bot-check is a completely different code path (`AuthRegister.tsx`'s Turnstile widget, checked before any passkey code runs) from the login report, and this one *was* reproducible by code review alone. Root cause: Cloudflare Turnstile tokens are single-use — `TurnstileWidget.tsx` fetched one token on mount and held it in React state indefinitely, with `window.turnstile.reset()` (present on the component's own TypeScript declaration) never called anywhere in the codebase. Any failed `/api/register` submission — a genuine bot-check failure, or *any other* validation error like a duplicate email — consumes that token at Cloudflare's end regardless of why our own logic rejected it, so every retry after the very first failure was guaranteed to fail the same bot check again, forever, with no recovery short of a full page reload. Exactly matches "Bot check failed. Please try again" being shown to someone who evidently *did* try again. Fixed by exposing a `reset()` method off `TurnstileWidget` (`forwardRef`/`useImperativeHandle`, calling the already-declared-but-unused `window.turnstile.reset(widgetId)`) and calling it — along with clearing the stale token from state — in `AuthRegister.tsx`'s catch block, but only on the branch where the account wasn't actually created yet (`!accountCreated`); the passkey-ceremony-retry branch is untouched since `createAccountOnce()` already short-circuits past `/api/register` once an account exists, so Turnstile staleness never applies there. `TurnstileWidget` has exactly one call site (`AuthRegister.tsx`), confirmed by grep, so no other flow needed updating. **Verified this one live and completely**, unlike most fixes this session: since this sandbox has no route to Cloudflare's real Turnstile challenge, injected a faithful mock of `window.turnstile` (`render`/`reset`/callback semantics matching the real API) into a real browser session driving the real `/register` UI, plus a routed `/api/register` that fails the first submission and only accepts the second if it carries a *different* token than the first. Confirmed end-to-end: attempt 1 fails and shows the error, `turnstile.reset()` fires automatically, the mock delivers a fresh token, attempt 2 succeeds using that new token with zero user action beyond clicking "try again." Also verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` (clean), `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts` (7 passed, 1 appropriately skipped). | — | ✅ Done (code) |
| 163 | src/app/page.tsx | **"Index still doesn't fit" (2026-07-07, screenshot attached) — mobile horizontal overflow on the homepage hero.** This one was actually reproducible live in this sandbox, unlike the DB-backed pages this session keeps flagging as code-review-only: `/`'s stat counts are already wrapped in `.catch(() => 0)`, so pointing the dev server at an unreachable placeholder `DATABASE_URL` renders the real page with `0` stats instead of crashing — used Playwright at a 390px viewport to measure `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` directly rather than guess from the screenshot. Root cause: the hero's headline is hard-broken into three `<br/>`-separated lines ("Independent" / "music built" / "for the scene.") with no `overflow-wrap`, and "Independent" is a single unbreakable word. On a narrow phone the CSS Grid item wrapping the hero copy (`gridTemplateColumns: '1fr auto'`, a leftover 2-column layout with only one column ever populated) has the browser-default `min-width: auto`, which resolves to that word's full min-content width — confirmed by instrumenting the live DOM: the grid's `1fr` track was computing to `479.86px` inside a `350px` container, a classic "CSS Grid blowout." Fixing only the grid sizing (`min-width: 0` on the item) shrank the track but the still-unbreakable word simply rendered past its new, narrower box instead (`overflow: visible` is the default) — confirmed empirically that `min-width` alone left `scrollWidth` unchanged at `500` vs a `390` viewport. Only combining `min-width: 0` on the grid item *and* `overflow-wrap: break-word` on `.idx-hero-h1` actually closed the gap — reverified live, `scrollWidth` dropped to exactly `390`, matching `clientWidth`. Tried `hyphens: auto` first for a cleaner mid-word break than a raw character split, but it didn't visibly hyphenate in this sandbox's headless Chromium (no hyphenation dictionary installed) so it was dropped rather than ship something unverifiable — the plain `break-word` result ("Independe-/nt") is blunter than a real hyphen but has zero overflow and matches standard mobile-web fallback behavior for a word that can't fit at any reasonable hero font size (even the mobile clamp's floor, 2.5rem, isn't small enough to fit "Independent" in the ~350px content width without breaking it). Full-page rescan after the fix confirmed the only remaining elements whose raw geometry exceeds the viewport (`.idx-role-card`, `.site-background-orb-a/b`) are correctly contained by their own intentional `overflow-x:auto` carousel and `overflow:hidden` background wrapper respectively — not real page-level overflow, and out of scope. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (204 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` (clean), `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts` (7 passed, 1 appropriately skipped), **and**, unusually for this session, a real rendered-in-browser confirmation at 390×844 showing `scrollWidth === clientWidth` before shipping — not code-review-only. | — | ✅ Done (code) |

Needs to complete flow and save to database then allow editing going forward." / "Accessibility doesn't let you scroll down to apply. Language change doesn't actually change anything either. Advertise page still doesn't fit."** **Broken deploy (found, not reported):** two Dependabot PRs (#452/#453) had merged since row 161 and bumped `prisma`/`@prisma/client`/`@prisma/adapter-pg` 6→7, `zod` 3→4, `stripe` 17→22, `bcryptjs` 2→3, `typescript` 5→6 in one shot — Prisma 7 rejects `schema.prisma`'s inline `url`/`directUrl` datasource fields (removed in v7, moved to `prisma.config.ts`), so `prisma generate` failed outright, which cascaded into ~300 TypeScript errors sitewide (every `db.*` call losing its types) and a failing `deploy-production.yml` run on `main`. Asked the user whether to revert the majors or migrate forward; **user chose migrate.** Fixed properly, not by pinning back: removed `url`/`directUrl` from `schema.prisma`'s datasource block (kept just `provider = "postgresql"`, per Prisma's own v7 upgrade guide); added a `datasource: { url: process.env.DATABASE_URL }` block to `prisma.config.ts`, reusing its own already-existing multi-provider env-var resolution logic (`db.ts`'s runtime client already used the `@prisma/adapter-pg` driver-adapter pattern v7 requires — that half needed no changes). Prisma 7's `@prisma/client` package also dropped the `/wasm` subpath entirely (collapsed into one universal entry resolved per-runtime via package.json `exports` conditions) — updated all 17 source files importing `@prisma/client/wasm` to import from `@prisma/client` directly. Stripe v22's SDK types pin a newer API version literal than the codebase's hardcoded `'2025-02-24.acacia'` — updated both `new Stripe(...)` call sites (`lib/stripe.ts`, `api/ads/stripe-webhook/route.ts`) to `'2026-06-24.dahlia'` (uncheckable against live Stripe in this sandbox, but a mechanical, spec-required pin bump, not a behavior change to any call). One more casualty surfaced by the churn: `admin-api-guard.test.ts` imports `glob` directly but it was never a declared dependency — just a phantom transitive one that happened to get hoisted before; the version reshuffle stopped hoisting it (`Cannot find module 'glob'`) — added it as an explicit devDependency. zod v4 and bcryptjs v3 needed no code changes at all — this codebase's usage of both (`.issues` not `.errors`, standard `.hash`/`.compare`) was already v4/v3-compatible. Verified the actual failing CI path end-to-end, not just `next build`: `tsc --noEmit` (clean, ~300 errors → 0), `vitest run` (204 passed), `next build --webpack` (clean), and `npm run cf:build` (the literal Cloudflare Worker bundle step `deploy-production.yml` runs — succeeds, confirming `@opennextjs/cloudflare` 1.7→1.20 and the worker-bundle patch script are also unaffected). **"Add page" flow didn't visibly complete:** row 161 built a real `POST /api/profiles` + inline creation form, and it does save to the database — confirmed no logic bug (traced the full path again: create → `refreshAll()` → `selectedPageId` → `selectedProfile` resolves → `PageEditor` renders). The actual gap was pure UX: on success the create-card just silently collapsed back to the plain 3-card grid with zero confirmation, while the new "EDITING" + `PageEditor` section rendered off-screen above the fold (the user was scrolled down to reach the Creator cards) — indistinguishable from "nothing happened." Added a scroll-to-top-of-tab (`contentTopRef.current.scrollIntoView`, reusing the same ref every tab-switch already scrolls to) plus a 6-second "✓ '[name]' page created — saved to your account. Edit it below." banner. Also hardened against any refetch-timing edge case by optimistically merging the created profile into local state immediately (`setData` merge) rather than relying solely on the `refreshAll()` round-trip to have it ready before `selectedProfile` resolves. **Accessibility panel — can't scroll to Apply:** the mobile panel's `max-height: calc(100vh - 92px)` is the classic iOS Safari bug — `100vh` there means the *collapsed-chrome* viewport height, not what's currently visible when the address bar/bottom toolbar are showing (as in the screenshot), so the box's real height (and its Reset/Apply footer) rendered below the actually-visible screen, under Safari's own toolbar. Added `max-height: calc(100dvh - 92px)` as a progressive-enhancement override (declared after the `vh` line so unsupported browsers just keep the old value) plus `-webkit-overflow-scrolling: touch` for reliable momentum-scroll inside the fixed sheet. **Language selector — does nothing:** traced exactly what it did — set `document.documentElement.lang`/a `dataset` attribute only, with its own help text already admitting *"Updates the page language metadata for screen readers and browser translation tools"* — this app has zero real translated content or i18n system anywhere (confirmed via the same grep sweep used throughout this session), so switching it never changed anything visible, matching the report exactly. Building actual Spanish/French/Portuguese translations for the whole site is a real feature, not a bug fix, and there's no source-of-truth copy to translate from — removed the dropdown entirely (`AccessibilitySettings.language` field, `languageOptions`, the `<label className="a11y-language">` block, and its now-orphaned CSS) rather than ship a control that visibly does nothing; `<html lang="en">` was already set statically in `layout.tsx` regardless, so no real a11y metadata capability was lost. Flagging this as a legitimate future ask if real multi-language support is wanted — that would need actual translated strings supplied, not invented here. **Advertise still doesn't fit:** a fourth instance of this session's recurring class in `AdvertisePage.tsx` (rows 159/160) — the "HYPE Screen" scanner demo's actual 2-column grid (`.86fr 1.14fr`, `AIScanner`'s own root div) had no class at all, while `.adv-guard` — already listed in the existing `@media (max-width:1040px)` collapse rule — sat on an inert wrapper `<div>` one level up with no styling of its own. Moved the class onto the real grid element and deleted the now-pointless wrapper; no new CSS needed, the correct rule already existed and just couldn't reach its target. Verified all four fixes via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (204 passed), `next build --webpack` (clean), `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts` (7 passed, 1 appropriately skipped). **Not verified live:** same recurring sandbox limitation — the add-page completion UX, the Accessibility `dvh` fix, and the Advertise `AIScanner` class fix all need a live DB/real iOS Safari to see rendered, and were verified by code review only. | POST /api/profiles (unchanged, from row 161); no other API surface changes | ✅ Done (code) |

| 152 | useDragCarousel.ts; e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts | **Prevent side-to-side swiping inside an open sub-tab (user-requested, 2026-07-07): "When in sub menu (Listen>Charts, for example) prevent side to side swiping (except in Seeds)."** Root cause: `MobileAppShell.tsx`'s cross-section drag-carousel (`useDragCarousel`) attaches its touch listeners to `.mas-track` — the ancestor of every section's content, not just its grid overlay — and was enabled by `!!shell?.active` alone, with no check for whether a sub-tab was actually open. `MobileQuickGrid`'s own grid-overlay swipe is a *separate* mechanism (portaled to `document.body`, so it never reaches `.mas-track` at all) already correctly scoped to grid-mode only — but once a sub-tab like Listen > Charts was open, the grid overlay goes inert and any horizontal drag on the now-visible content fell straight through to `.mas-track`'s always-on listener, silently swiping to the next/previous section instead of whatever the sub-tab itself wanted to do with that gesture. Fixed by checking, at `touchstart`, whether the event's target is inside `.mqg-content:not(.is-hidden)` (every *Home component's own sub-tab content wrapper) and simply not arming the drag if so — grid-mode touches are unaffected (they never reach `.mas-track` in the first place, per above) and Seeds' card-deck gesture needed no special-casing at all: it's built on Pointer Events + `touch-action: none` (`ListenHome.tsx`), an entirely separate event system from the raw Touch events `useDragCarousel` listens for, so it was never fighting this gesture and isn't affected by the fix either way — "except in Seeds" turned out to already be true by construction once sub-tabs generally stopped hijacking the gesture. Added a new, verified-meaningful e2e case ("swiping inside an open sub-tab does not change section") — caught two of my own false-passes before it was solid: an initial version dispatched touch events with no pacing between steps and never reproduced the bug at all, and a second version raced Playwright's `.not.toHaveCSS` assertion against the (buggy) async `setState → history.pushState → re-render` chain, passing on a stale pre-update snapshot instead of actually proving nothing changed. Confirmed the final version genuinely fails against the reverted, pre-fix code (real regression coverage, not a tautology) before restoring the fix. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (194 passed, unchanged), `next build --webpack` (clean), and the full `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts` suite (7 passed, 1 appropriately skipped, x2 repeat for stability). | — | ✅ Done (code) |

| 146 | (new) native-push.ts, deep-link.ts, NativePushRegistration.tsx; api/push/register-device; .well-known/apple-app-site-association, .well-known/assetlinks.json; schema.prisma; capacitor.config.ts; AndroidManifest.xml; Info.plist | **Push notifications + deep-link scaffolding (user-requested, 2026-07-06: "Have it ready for export to iOS android apps and maintain as future updates are added."), following up on row 111's explicit "deliberately not scaffolded half-working" deferral.** Built everything that can be built and verified without external credentials; clearly documented (code comments + this row) exactly what still needs a human to create an account and flip a switch. **Server-side, fully real and tested:** new `NativeDeviceToken` model (userId/platform/token — distinct from the existing `PushSubscription`, which is Web Push VAPID-shaped, not an APNs/FCM device-token shape) + migration; `POST/DELETE /api/push/register-device` (session-gated, mirrors `/api/push/subscribe`'s device-cap/upsert pattern); new `sendNativePushNotification()` in `native-push.ts` — a hand-rolled Firebase Cloud Messaging HTTP v1 sender (Web Crypto RS256 JWT service-account exchange, no new npm dependency, matching `push-notify.ts`'s existing hand-rolled VAPID approach and staying Cloudflare-Workers-portable) chosen as a **single unified transport for both iOS and Android** — FCM proxies to APNs for iOS tokens once an APNs auth key is uploaded to the Firebase project, so there's no separate raw-APNs implementation to maintain going forward. Wired into `notify.ts`'s single `notifyUser()` fan-in point alongside the existing web-push call, so every existing notification trigger (hype milestones, promoter ticket sales, etc.) *and every future one* gets native push for free with zero additional wiring — directly answers "maintain as future updates are added." Safely no-ops (matching the existing VAPID pattern exactly) whenever `FCM_PROJECT_ID`/`FCM_CLIENT_EMAIL`/`FCM_PRIVATE_KEY` aren't set. **Client-side, native-shell-only:** new `NativePushRegistration.tsx`, mounted once in `AppProviders.tsx` — every code path is gated on `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()`, so it's a verified complete no-op on the website/PWA (confirmed: full e2e mobile-shell suite still green with it mounted). On the real native app it requests permission, registers for push, POSTs the real token to `/api/push/register-device`, and handles both a tapped-notification's `link` and a Universal/App Link's URL (via a new `@capacitor/app` `appUrlOpen` listener) by navigating the SPA there — both paths share `resolveInternalPath()` (`deep-link.ts`, pure + unit-tested, 8 cases including a subdomain-suffix spoofing rejection) so a malformed/spoofed URL can never navigate the app off-origin. **Deep-link association files, real but inert until configured:** new `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` and `/.well-known/assetlinks.json` routes, env-driven (`APPLE_TEAM_ID`, `ANDROID_CERT_SHA256_FINGERPRINTS`) — serve valid, correctly-shaped JSON with empty claims when unset (verified live: both endpoints return well-formed placeholders unset, and correctly populate real values when the env vars are provided), so they're genuinely ready the moment those accounts exist, not fake stand-ins. Added the Android App Links `intent-filter` (`autoVerify="true"`, declarative XML, doesn't require the real cert fingerprint to compile) and the iOS `UIBackgroundModes: remote-notification` Info.plist key (harmless without the entitlement) — both are safe, additive, non-signing-dependent native-project changes. **Deliberately not attempted, too risky to hand-edit blind:** wiring Push Notifications + Associated Domains capabilities into `ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` — Xcode's project file format isn't safe to hand-edit without Xcode itself to verify the result, and both capabilities are inert without a real Apple Developer Program Team ID/provisioning profile anyway (same blocker as row 111's signed build job). Also deliberately not touched: `android/build.gradle`'s Google Services plugin — it requires a real `google-services.json` from a Firebase project to even compile, and adding it now would break the currently-passing `android-debug-build` CI check for zero present benefit. **What a human still needs to do, in order:** (1) create a free Firebase project, download its service-account JSON, set `FCM_PROJECT_ID`/`FCM_CLIENT_EMAIL`/`FCM_PRIVATE_KEY`; (2) place the project's `google-services.json` in `android/app/` and apply the Google Services Gradle plugin (2-line change, safe once the file exists); (3) once a paid Apple Developer Program account/Team ID exists, upload an APNs auth key to that same Firebase project and add the Push Notifications + Associated Domains capabilities via Xcode's Signing & Capabilities tab (auto-wires the entitlements file correctly); (4) set `APPLE_TEAM_ID` and `ANDROID_CERT_SHA256_FINGERPRINTS` once those exist. All four steps are additive to what's shipped here — no code changes required, just configuration. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (194 passed — added 8 new `resolveInternalPath` tests), `next build --webpack` (confirms both `.well-known` routes and the new API route compile as real routes), the full `e2e/mobile-shell.spec.ts` suite (6 passed, 1 appropriately skipped — confirms the new always-mounted `NativePushRegistration` component doesn't affect the website), and live dev-server checks of both `.well-known` endpoints (empty-placeholder and populated-via-env-var states) and the register-device route's 401 when unauthenticated. **Not verified, and can't be from this sandbox:** an actual native build with the new plugins (`@capacitor/push-notifications`, `@capacitor/app`) — no Xcode/Android SDK here; that's what `native-build.yml`'s existing debug-build CI jobs are for, and they'll run for real on the next push to this branch. | POST/DELETE /api/push/register-device (new), GET /.well-known/apple-app-site-association (new), GET /.well-known/assetlinks.json (new) | ✅ Done (code) / ⏳ Needs a Firebase project + (for iOS delivery and Universal Links) an Apple Developer Program account |

| 137 | ListenHome, EventsHome, PagesHome, MobileQuickGrid, MobileShellContext, MobileAppShell | **Allow swiping between main tabs while viewing a section's grid/home screen (user-requested, 2026-07-06: "Allow swiping between main tabs").** The persistent shell's horizontal drag-carousel (`useDragCarousel`, row 126) already worked, but only once drilled into a tab's content — `MobileQuickGrid`'s grid/home overlay is portaled straight to `document.body` (so it can render above everything with its own z-index), which puts it on a completely separate real-DOM branch from `MobileAppShell`'s `.mas-track`. Native `addEventListener` bubbling follows the actual DOM tree, not the React tree, so `.mas-track`'s touch listeners physically could never see touches that started on the portaled grid overlay — meaning swiping never worked on the exact screen (each section's grid "home") users see most of the time. Fixed by giving `MobileQuickGrid` its own lightweight swipe detection directly on the portaled overlay (axis-locked start/move/end tracking, reusing the existing pure `resolveDragCommit` from `mobileShell.ts` for the same 20%-of-width commit threshold the content-view carousel already uses) via a new optional `onSwipeSection` prop. Rather than duplicate the "which section is next/prev" math in a second place, moved it out of `MobileAppShell`'s local `handleCommit` and into `MobileShellContext` as a new `swipeSection(direction)` method (single source of truth, also now used by `MobileAppShell` itself); each `*Home` component reads it via `useMobileShell()` and passes it straight through to its own `MobileQuickGrid` call. Deliberately not a live drag-follow like the content-view carousel — it's a simple detect-on-release gesture, so on commit `shell.setSection()` changes and the existing `.mas-slot` transform animates smoothly to the new section (same `.32s cubic-bezier` transition already in place), just without the finger-tracking motion. Scoped safely: since the gesture handlers live only on the portaled overlay div itself (not `document`/`window`), it can't accidentally hijack swipes over the bottom nav, an open `NavDrawer`, or any other unrelated on-screen element. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (175 passed), `next build --webpack`; not visually re-verified on a real device (no live DB/browser session in this sandbox). | — | ✅ Done (code) |
| 136 | ListenHome, EventsHome, PagesHome | **Mobile UX follow-ups from a "how else can the mobile view be improved?" audit (Claude Code, 2026-07-06) — user replied "Do all" to 3 candidates.** (1) **Pull-to-refresh, actually wired up:** `globals.css` already had an orphaned `.pull-to-refresh-spinner.spinning` keyframe rule with zero component ever referencing it — built the missing `PullToRefresh.tsx` (native-style: arms only when `window.scrollY === 0`, tracks touch delta with resistance, shows/rotates the existing spinner glyph, calls `onRefresh` past a 64px threshold) and wrapped each module's `.mqg-content` in it, wiring `onRefresh` to each component's own mount-time data fetch (extracted into a reusable `refreshAll`/`refreshDirectory`+`refreshTickets` callback instead of an inline one-shot effect). (2) **Cross-section scroll preservation — audited, already correct, no change needed:** `MobileAppShell`'s `.mas-slot` (one per always-mounted Listen/Events/Pages section) is `position:absolute; overflow-y:auto` and never unmounts, so each section's scroll position already survives swiping away and back — confirmed this was a deliberate existing design (see row 121/126), not a gap. (3) **Real gap found instead: in-section tab switches didn't reset scroll.** Neither the tabstrip pills nor the initial grid→tab tap ever reset `.mas-slot`'s (or, off-shell, the window's) scroll position, so scrolling deep into one tab (e.g. Seeds) and switching to another (e.g. Charts) could land mid-scroll or blank instead of at the top. Fixed with a zero-height sentinel div at the top of each `.mqg-content` plus a `scrollIntoView({block:'start'})` effect keyed on `tab` — works for both the shell's `.mas-slot` scroll container and the plain-page/desktop case without needing to know which ancestor is actually scrolling. **Considered and explicitly declined: converting the sitewide `<img>` usage to `next/image`.** Found the real gap (11 files still on raw `<img>`), but investigation showed `avatarImage`/`galleryImage`/`artworkUrl` are free-text fields (`profile-editor`'s `urlText = z.string().trim().max(2048)`, no protocol/host validation) — a user can point their avatar at any arbitrary `https://` URL, and `next/image` hard-errors on any host not in `next.config.js`'s `remotePatterns` (locked to `ihype.org`/`*.ihype.org` on purpose — the same file's own comment warns a `'**'` wildcard "turns `/_next/image` into an open proxy"). Every one of these `<img>` call sites already carries a deliberate `eslint-disable-next-line @next/next/no-img-element`, confirming this was a prior considered choice, not an oversight — converting them now would trade a performance nicety for a real breakage (any profile with a non-ihype.org avatar 500s) or a real open-proxy risk (widening the wildcard). Left as-is; flagging here in case a scoped `next/image`-with-safe-host-fallback wrapper is wanted later. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (175 passed), `next build --webpack`; not visually re-verified on a real device (no live DB/browser session in this sandbox). | — (client-side re-fetch of existing GETs) | ✅ Done (code) |

Added `resetTokens`/`goToSectionHome()` to `MobileShellContext`: `goToSectionHome(section)` switches section and bumps a per-section counter; `MobileAppShell` passes each section's current counter value to its `*Home` component as a new `resetToken` prop; each of `ListenHome`/`EventsHome`/`PagesHome` watches `resetToken` (via a ref-guarded effect that ignores the initial mount value, so it never fights the existing `initialTab` deep-link logic) and calls `setGridMode(true)` when it changes. `MobileBottomNav`'s tab `onClick` now calls `shell.goToSectionHome()` instead of `shell.setSection()` — every bottom-nav tap is now a genuine "go to this section's home" action, a second, more obvious way back to the grid alongside the small in-content back button. Retired `useSwipeRouteNav.ts` and its tests entirely — superseded by the shell's drag-follow, and keeping both would have double-handled the same touch gesture. Reused the existing scroll-lock mechanism from row 123 via a new small reference-counted `scrollLock.ts` helper, since both a section's own quick-grid and the shell's outer container now need to hold the lock without one releasing early and undoing the other's. **Known limitations, not fully verifiable without a real device:** browser back/forward across shell-only (pushState-driven) section changes may fall back to a real page navigation rather than a soft one; there may be a brief hydration flash on first mobile load before the shell takes over from the routed page content; the hamburger drawer's own active-link highlighting (`usePathname()`-based) can be briefly stale immediately after a shell-driven section change. | 123 | MobileQuickGrid | **Mobile quick-grid overscroll-bounce fix (Claude Code, 2026-07-04), user-reported ("Infinite scroll not working. Button isn't fixed in size (I pulled down and the buttons elongated and it looks bad)").** Follow-up to row 122's containing-block fix — that fixed positioning relative to the true viewport, but nothing stopped the page underneath the overlay from still rubber-band bouncing on iOS Safari when the user dragged down from the top: `useSwipeRouteNav`'s touch listeners are `passive: true` and only handle `touchstart`/`touchend` (no `preventDefault`), so they never blocked the native elastic overscroll. That bounce is a known WebKit rendering bug — during the bounce, fixed-position elements can visibly stretch/distort even though their `top`/`bottom` values never change. Fixed by locking scroll on `<html>`/`<body>` (`overflow: hidden; overscroll-behavior: none`) via a `mqg-scroll-lock` class toggled in a `useEffect` while the grid overlay is `active`, plus `overscroll-behavior: none; touch-action: pan-x` directly on `.mqg-overlay` itself. This matches the grid's original design intent (no scrolling at all — an edge-to-edge, screen-filling grid) rather than adding new scroll behavior. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (172 passed), and `next build`; not visually re-verified on a real device. | — | ✅ Done (code) |
| 122 | MobileQuickGrid, layout.tsx | **Mobile quick-grid containing-block fix + PWA prompt removal + footer cleanup (Claude Code, 2026-07-04), user-reported ("Squares not right things bottling up right" / "Still not aligned also drop the add to home screen thing - too busy" / "Remove text footer as it exists in mobile menu").** Real bug from row 121's grid, confirmed via two device screenshots (not a caching issue — reproduced identically after a genuine reload): `globals.css`'s `.site-shell > main { animation: page-enter 0.4s ... both; }` leaves a permanent non-`none` `transform` on `<main>` (fill-mode `both` keeps the final keyframe's transform applied after the animation ends), and any non-`none` transform on an ancestor makes it the containing block for `position:fixed` descendants — so `MobileQuickGrid`'s `.mqg-overlay`, rendered inside `<main>{children}</main>`, was never actually pinned to the true viewport, just to `<main>`'s own box, causing the grid rows to compress/overlap with the real footer showing through underneath. Fixed by having `MobileQuickGrid` render via `createPortal` into `document.body` (gated on a `mounted` state for SSR safety) so it escapes `<main>`'s containing block entirely, matching how every other fixed-position element in the app (bottom nav, cookie banner, player dock) already avoids this by living in `layout.tsx` outside `<main>`. Also removed the `PwaInstallPrompt` "Add iHYPE to your home screen" banner from `layout.tsx` (and deleted the now-unused component) per explicit "too busy" feedback, and hid the desktop text footer (`SiteFooter`'s Advertise/Legal/Support links + copyright line) on mobile (`@media max-width:768px`) since those links are redundant with the hamburger drawer's secondary link list. Verified via `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run` (172 passed), and `next build` (all routes compile clean); not visually re-verified on a real device (no live DB session for Playwright in this sandbox). | — | ✅ Done (code) |

| 117 | (audit only, no design change) | **Sitewide security + accessibility audit (Claude Code, 2026-07-04), user-requested ("ensure all accessibility and security requirements are embedded").** Security: found and fixed a real stored-XSS vulnerability — `src/app/shows/[slug]/page.tsx`'s JSON-LD `<script>` tag interpolated `JSON.stringify(jsonLd)` (built from user-controlled show title/description/profile names) straight into `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, and `JSON.stringify` does not escape `</script>`, so a title containing that sequence could break out and execute arbitrary script for every visitor. Fixed via a new `toSafeJsonLdString()` helper (`src/lib/safe-json-ld.ts`, unit-tested) that escapes the `<` character to its unicode-escaped form before it reaches the DOM. Also closed the one real gap in the existing cookie-consent flow: the CF Insights beacon in `layout.tsx` loaded unconditionally regardless of the user's cookie choice — new `AnalyticsBeacon.tsx` client component gates it on `localStorage` + a `ihype:cookie-consent` event the banner now dispatches, so it never loads until "Accept all" is chosen. Everything else audited came back clean: `next.config.mjs` already ships a comprehensive header set (HSTS, CSP, X-Frame-Options, COOP/CORP, etc.) applied to every route including `/api/*`; all public unauthenticated write endpoints already rate-limited; all three webhook receivers (Stripe ×2, Resend) verify signatures; session cookies are `httpOnly`+`sameSite:lax`+`secure` (adequate CSRF mitigation); no hardcoded secrets in source; `.env*` properly gitignored; `npm audit` clean apart from one low-severity, Windows-only, dev-tooling-only `esbuild` transitive advisory. New `AccountsPayableEntry.stripeTransferId` records the real transfer. **(2) Ticket transfer — real double-use fix.** Transferring a ticket used to just email the recipient the *existing* serializedId while leaving the `Ticket` row (status, holder) completely untouched — since every ticket lookup (the public QR page, both scan endpoints) has no ownership check beyond the id itself, the original buyer (who may have already opened, screenshotted, or printed the QR before initiating the transfer) still held a fully valid, scannable copy; whoever scanned first won, a real race. Now reissues every ticket in the order with a brand-new `serializedId` (`createSerializedTicketId()`) inside a `$transaction`, updates `holderName`/`holderEmail` to the recipient, and increments the existing `reassignCount`/`reassignedAt` fields (already used by the separate venue-staff "Venue reassignment" feature on `/tickets/[serializedId]` — reused rather than inventing parallel fields). Wired into `profile/upload-graphic` (flagged upload still stores, but raises a `ContentReport` `targetType: 'profile-image'`) and `ads/upload` (flagged creative forces `AdSubmission.status` to `manual_review` even when the ad copy itself passed text vetting, plus raises `ContentReport` `targetType: 'ad-creative'`). **(2) Admin page with actionable modules — two real gaps fixed, not a rebuild.** `/admin` was already a large 1041-line dashboard with 8 nav sections (`AdminShell.tsx`) covering the bulk of ops work, so "all-encompassing" meant closing the two things that made it *not* all-encompassing: (a) audited every one of the 16 `src/app/admin/*` directories and found `flagged`/`verifications` are dead — `loading.tsx` only, no `page.tsx`, not real routes — while `audit`, `feedback`, `journal`, and `playlists` **are** real, populated pages with zero inbound link anywhere in the app (reachable only by typing the URL); added a `SUBNAV` strip to `AdminShell.tsx` (renders under the section a page belongs to, per `sectionFromPathname()`, which itself needed a `playlists → growth` mapping it was missing) so every real subpage is now a click away — `device-register` stays deliberately unlinked, it's only ever reached via an emailed one-time-token link during device re-registration, not a nav destination. (b) The moderation queue's "Remove content" button (`ModerationActions.tsx` → `PATCH /api/admin/moderation/[id]`) was cosmetic — it only flipped `ContentReport.status` to `ACTIONED` and never touched the flagged content itself. Added `enforceRemoval()`, keyed by `targetType`: `track` unpublishes the `ArtistMediaAsset` (and clears its free-use flag), `comment` soft-deletes the `ShowComment` (existing `deletedAt` column), `show` cancels the `Show`, `ad-creative` rejects the `AdSubmission`. `profile`/`profile-image` still have no automated action — a flagged profile doesn't say which field was the problem, so an admin has to look; stated explicitly rather than guessing at something destructive. Also added `profile-image`/`ad-creative` to the moderation page's type filter so the new report categories are actually browsable. **(3) Public audit page.** New `/audit` — distinct from `/transparency` (financial: artist/show/ticket counts, $ paid out) and `/status` (system health, not touched this pass — still has no auth gate, a separate pre-existing concern flagged for a future session, not this one's scope). `/audit` is a Trust & Safety report: aggregate `ContentReport` counts (total/open/actioned/dismissed), a per-category breakdown, ad-vetting outcome counts, and verified-profile counts — all real DB aggregates, zero PII, zero content/user IDs. Applied the same k-anonymity floor already established elsewhere in this codebase (row 88's "cohorts under 5 fans are never shown" for the Recommend tab): any `ContentReport` category with fewer than 5 reports is folded into an "Other" bucket instead of shown with an exact count, so a bucket of 1–2 reports can never be used to infer which specific piece of content or user was involved. Linked from `NavDrawer.tsx` (new "Trust & Safety" entry next to "Transparency") and added to `sitemap-static.xml`. tsc clean, vitest 260/260 (unchanged — no new pure-logic module needed a dedicated test file; `image-vetting.ts`/`audit/page.tsx` are thin AI-call/DB-aggregate wrappers, same testing posture as `ad-vetting.ts`/`transparency/page.tsx`), source-policy lint clean (616 files), guard:design clean, production build succeeds (`/audit` compiles as a dynamic route). **Not runtime-verified against a live DB or the live Workers AI binding** — same sandbox limitation as every prior row; `runVisionAI`'s exact response shape (`description` vs `response` field) is based on Cloudflare's published docs for `@cf/llava-hf/llava-1.5-7b-hf`, not a live call, so recommend a real image-upload smoke test once deployed to confirm the vision model's actual output field name. | PATCH /api/admin/moderation/[id] (now performs real enforcement), POST /api/profile/upload-graphic (vetting added, no shape change), POST /api/ads/upload (vetting added, status can now land on manual_review from image alone) | ✅ Done (code) |
| 201 | src/app/api/auth/signout/route.ts (NEW), src/app/settings/page.tsx | **Fix: "Sign out doesn't work" (user report, 2026-07-12).** `/api/auth/signout` had no dedicated route — it fell through to the `[...nextauth]` catch-all. But sessions here are built by hand (`buildAuthSessionCookie`, not NextAuth's signIn flow), and Auth.js's signout only clears the session on a **CSRF POST** — a GET just renders a confirmation page. So the "Sign out" links (all plain GET navigations) did nothing. Fix: (1) new `src/app/api/auth/signout/route.ts` (GET **and** POST) that expires the exact custom session cookie `getAuthSessionCookieName()` (`__Secure-authjs.session-token` in prod / `authjs.session-token` in dev) plus its chunked `.0`/`.1` variants, with matching options (path `/`, httpOnly, secure, sameSite lax, maxAge 0), then redirects to `/`; a static route takes precedence over the catch-all, which still serves every other `/api/auth/*` path. Session strategy is JWT (stateless), so clearing the cookie fully signs out — no DB session to invalidate, and no `userSecurityVersion` bump (that would sign out every device). (2) `settings/page.tsx` Danger-Zone "Sign out" changed from `<Link>` to a plain `<a>` — a soft client-side nav to an API route won't actually hit it. NavDrawer's signout `<a>` and settings' `window.location.href` were already hard navigations and now resolve correctly. tsc, vitest 254/254, source-policy lint, design guard clean. **Not runtime-verified** (DB/auth blocker) — standard auth-cookie-clearing pattern; recommend a quick signed-in→Sign out click once deployed. | 2026-07-12 |

| 198 | src/components/AuthRegister.tsx | **Fix: iOS mobile Safari couldn't complete passkey registration (alpha-user bug report + screenshot, 2026-07-12: "couldn't register with passkey or magic link from iOS").** Root cause (screenshot showed the passkey step stuck on "Waiting for your device passkey prompt" → "Challenge expired"): the passkey ceremony did `await fetch(register-first)` + `await res.json()` **before** `startRegistration()`. iOS Safari only runs `navigator.credentials.create()` inside the **transient user activation** from a tap; an awaited network fetch between the tap and the ceremony consumes that activation, so the Face ID / passkey sheet silently never appears (Chromium/Android are lenient, which is why it worked elsewhere and wasn't caught). Fix (client-only, all backend wiring — endpoints, bootstrap cookie, server challenge, POST verification — unchanged): **pre-fetch the WebAuthn challenge into a ref** (`preparePasskeyOptions`) as soon as we land on the passkey step, so the "Try passkey again" tap calls `startRegistration()` with the options already in hand — making the ceremony the first awaited call after the gesture, which keeps iOS's activation valid. `registerPasskeyForAccount` gained an optional `prefetched` param (`prefetched ?? await fetchPasskeyOptions()`), `retryPasskey` consumes the warmed options and re-arms a fresh one on failure, and the initial auto-attempt (still inline-fetch, fine on Android/desktop) now warms options in its catch so the retry tap is reliable. Magic-link path was reviewed and is correct (send route + Lax session cookie on the top-level link nav both fine) — the reported magic-link failure is most likely email delivery (infra), not code. **Not device-verified** — the iOS-specific user-activation behavior can't be reproduced in headless Chromium and the app needs DB/auth to run; the fix follows the standard WebAuthn user-activation pattern and is provably no-worse on other platforms (unchanged inline-fetch path). tsc, vitest 250/250, source-policy lint, design guard clean. **Recommend a real iOS Safari signup test once deployed.** | 2026-07-12 |

Same proven method as the ink pass: a **theme-aware `--hair-<permille>` white-alpha scale** (22 tokens: `--hair-15`…`--hair-280`) whose **dark values are byte-identical** to the literals replaced (`rgba(255,255,255,op)`), with light values = `rgba(0,0,0,op)` at the same opacity so dark-surface hairlines become faint dark ones instead of disappearing; `.06`/`.14` reuse the existing `--line`/`--line-2`. **Deliberate scope boundary — only opacities ≤ 0.28** (unambiguous surface treatments) were tokenized; the 31 **high-opacity whites (≥ 0.3)** were left literal because those are white text/elements sitting on accent/colored fills that stay tinted in light mode, so flipping them to black would be wrong — a case-by-case call not safe to automate blind. Same allowlist + protections as before (OG/Satori, email builders, standalone-HTML routes, global-error all excluded and verified untouched; CSS custom-property definition lines protected). With this, the three-part light-theme fix (chrome → ink text → hairlines/fills) is complete. | 2026-07-11 |

Per-line protection skips CSS custom-property *definition* lines so the token defs themselves stay literal. Also collapsed 10 `var(--ink, #f0ebe5)`→`var(--ink, var(--ink))` fallback artifacts to `var(--ink)`. **Deliberately still literal:** misc white-alpha tints/borders (`rgba(255,255,255,.02/.03/.07/.08…)`) with no exact existing token — a smaller remaining light-mode drift for borders/bg-fills (far less severe than the text bug fixed here) that could get its own token scale later. tsc, vitest (250/250), source-policy lint, design guard all clean; globals.css brace balance 1234/1234. Extends PR #495 (`claude/visual-consistency-pass`). **Not runtime-verified** — same DB/auth blocker; a light-mode toggle check on a running instance is recommended, and CI's browser-smoke covers the default dark render (which is provably unchanged). | 2026-07-11 |
| 195 | src/app/globals.css | **Sitewide visual-consistency pass — fix the shipped-but-broken light theme (user request, 2026-07-11: "Improve app visually" → scoped via question to "Sitewide consistency pass: hardcoded colors vs tokens, spacing/inconsistency drift").** Audit finding: a live `ThemeToggle` (mounted in `NavDrawer`, writes `data-theme` on `<html>`) ships to users, and `[data-theme="light"]` tokens are defined — but two later plain `body { }` rules **hardcode a dark gradient background and near-white text**, and those override the light `--bg`/`--ink` tokens by source order, so toggling to light mode produced dark-on-light broken rendering across the whole app. Fixes, all additive in globals.css and **dark mode byte-identical by construction** (only new `[data-theme="light"]` rules + `color-scheme` + one `::selection` color that already equalled the old literal in dark): (1) `[data-theme="light"] body` override — warm light gradient (mirrors the dark rule's two-brand-radials-over-vertical-base structure in cream tones) + `color: var(--ink)` so default/inherited copy is dark and readable; higher specificity (0,1,1) beats the later plain `body` rules (0,0,1) regardless of order. (3) `::selection` text tokenized `#f0ebe5`→`var(--ink)` (identical in dark, readable in light). (2) **Subprocessor disclosure**: `/legal`'s Privacy tab gained a "Subprocessors" section naming Stripe, Supabase, Cloudflare, Resend, and Sentry with what each holds — the prior copy described this only abstractly ("systems and staff that need it"). (3) **CI dependency gate**: `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` now runs on every PR, right after `npm ci`, so a future high/critical production vulnerability fails the build instead of depending on someone remembering to check manually — verified it passes clean today. (4) **`ADMIN_SETUP_SECRET` rotation guidance**: added a "Standing secret hygiene" section to `docs/runbooks/incident-response.md` — this bearer secret has no built-in expiry (unlike the 20-minute bootstrap tokens it issues), so it now has an explicit rotate-after-use-and-quarterly-regardless cadence. (1) **G-1 identity detachment is now real**, not just promised: new `src/lib/privacy-actions.ts` adds `scrubAgedAuditLogIps()` run by a new daily `identity-detach` cron job (IP-only scrub of AuditLog rows past the 30-day default — actor linkage kept for security auditing, deleted entirely at 90 days by the existing rotate job) and `executeIdentityDetach()`/`executeHypeWipe()` which now run **instantly** when a user hits "detach now" / "wipe hype history" (previously these only filed a support ticket for someone to act on later — the ticket is still filed, but pre-marked DONE). (2) **G-3 admin execution tooling**: `executeAccountErasure()` is anonymization-based (never a hard `user.delete()`, which would cascade-destroy other people's ticket/payout history through the Show.creator relation) — deletes purely-personal rows (sessions, passkeys, follows, RSVPs, etc.), scrubs PII embedded in retained financial records (TicketOrder buyer fields → `deleted@ihype.invalid`, Ticket holder fields, EmailDeliveryLog recipient), anonymizes owned Profiles in place (name/bio/media wiped, slug rewritten, R2 media assets actually deleted via `deleteMediaFile`), and reduces the User row to an inert shell with `userSecurityVersion` bumped to kill every session; refuses outright on ADMIN accounts. Wired to a new passkey-reauth-gated admin route (`/api/admin/privacy-requests/[id]`) and a console action component surfaced directly on the existing support-requests panel — deletion requests stay `OPEN` for a human to click "Execute erasure" (with a confirm() guard), detach/hype-wipe rows show up already `DONE`. (3) **G-2 backup-verify** now cross-checks `_prisma_migrations` parity and audit-log write-freshness (a pure row-count can't catch silent write failures) and flags the 1st of each month as "restore drill due"; added `docs/runbooks/backup-restore-drill.md` for the actual Supabase-PITR-to-a-fork drill no cron can run unattended. (4) **G-6/G-7 runbooks**: `docs/runbooks/incident-response.md` (detection signals, containment via `userSecurityVersion` + secret rotation, the GDPR Art. 33 72-hour clock, evidence sources with their retention caveats, vendor contacts, user-notification template) and `docs/runbooks/pci-saq-a.md` (annual SAQ A checklist — the Stripe-dashboard attestation submission itself stays a human action). (5) **G-5**: `npm audit fix --dry-run` confirmed the 17 moderate advisories (transitively pinned `@opentelemetry/core` under dev-only `@sentry/nextjs`/`lighthouse`) have no non-breaking fix; left alone rather than force-upgrading. **SECURITY_COMPLIANCE.md** rewritten to mark G-1/G-2/G-3/G-5/G-6/G-7 closed with evidence, leaving only G-4 (accepted CSP risk) and G-8 (organizational-only: SOC2/ISO program, PCI SAQ submission itself) open. Added 6 unit tests for the new privacy-actions library (ADMIN-refusal, counter-decrement math, embedded-PII scrub, in-place profile anonymization) — 246/246 passing; tsc, source-policy lint, and design guard all clean. No UI redesign — SupportPrivacyPanel/Settings copy fixes only, same layout. | 2026-07-11 |

| 182 | src/app/api/profile-editor/route.ts, src/lib/profile-editor-schema.ts (NEW) | **Fixed "Invalid input" error saving Pages editor changes; investigated "where is the AI page customization engine?" (2026-07-09), both from the same user report.** Root cause of the save error: `PageEditor.tsx`'s `save()` PATCHes its *entire* client-side state back to `/api/profile-editor` on every save, including every field the initial `GET` returned as `null` (nearly all of the ~25 text fields, on any profile that hasn't filled in every field — i.e. almost every real profile). The Zod schema's `text()`/`urlText()` helpers were `z.string().trim().max(n).optional()` — `.optional()` permits `undefined` but explicitly not `null` — so the very first untouched null field failed validation, surfacing Zod v4's generic default message, `"Invalid input"`. Not Theme-tab-specific; it would fire from any tab, for any profile with any empty field. Fixed by adding `.nullable()` to both helpers and updating `emptyToNull()` to pass `null` through instead of only handling `string | undefined`. Extracted the schema out of `route.ts` into `src/lib/profile-editor-schema.ts` (route.ts pulls in `next-auth`/Prisma, which can't resolve under plain vitest — the same `@prisma/client/wasm` local-Node limitation hit elsewhere in this project — so the schema needed to live somewhere dependency-light to be unit-testable) and added a regression test (`profile-editor-schema.test.ts`) asserting a payload shaped like a real mostly-empty-profile GET response parses successfully; confirmed the test fails against the pre-fix schema and passes against the fix. **AI page customization engine: exists in the codebase, but is fully unwired from the UI.** `/api/page-builder/refine` (added 2026-07-03) sends a user's natural-language instruction plus the current theme JSON to Cloudflare Workers AI and returns structured theme-field changes (mood/font/layout/tagline/bio/palette); `/api/page-builder` (GET/PUT) stores a draft page JSON blob per profile (`Profile.pageDraft`/`pageDraftUpdatedAt`), `/api/page-builder/publish` promotes a draft to `pagePublished`/`pagePublishedAt`, and `/api/page-builder/ad-recs` exists alongside it. All four routes are real, working backend code with matching Prisma columns already migrated — but grepping the entire `src/` tree turns up zero client-side references to any of them; no component ever calls `/api/page-builder*`. It's backend-only and unreachable from the live app, same class of issue as the dormant theme-preset system `PageEditor.tsx` itself was built to surface. This needs a `.dc.html` design (per this repo's UI-source-of-truth rule) before a UI can be wired to it — flagging as a real, ready-to-build feature rather than vaporware. tsc clean, vitest 207/207 (206 prior + 1 new), guard:design clean. | — | ✅ Done (code) |

New "Stats" tab in `PageEditor.tsx` (role-filtered checkboxes, max 4), validated server-side against the profile's actual type in `PATCH /api/profile-editor` (a stale client can't pin a stat that doesn't apply to its role). Rendered via a new shared `PinnedStatTiles` component on all four real profile surfaces — Artist (About tab), Venue (About tab), the real DJ page at `/promoters/[slug]` (hero, no About tab there), and FanProfile (hero) — discovering along the way that `/artists/[slug]?role=dj` (CLAUDE.md's prior DJProfile mapping) was itself wrong; real DJ profiles live at `/promoters/[slug]`, fixed in CLAUDE.md. **(2) Upload content-moderation extended to all track uploads** — found real, already-shipped infrastructure this session's earlier audit missed: `vetFreeUseSample()` (`src/lib/media-vetting.ts`) already runs genuine Workers AI metadata vetting (copyright red flags, bootleg markers, hate speech, fail-open when AI unavailable), and `ContentReport` + `/admin/moderation` already form a complete, working admin review queue (the moderation page's type filter already had a `<option value="track">` — clearly anticipated, never wired) — but vetting only ran for uploads opted into the shared free-use radio crate, leaving the large majority of ordinary track uploads with zero automated screening. Extended `POST /api/artist-media` to vet every upload regardless of free-use status; a flagged track still publishes immediately (fail-open, unchanged behavior) but now also raises a `ContentReport` (`targetType: 'track'`) into the existing queue instead of leaving zero trace. This is the real, honest version of what the original Artist.dc.html design's `SCAN_LAYERS` UI implied — genuine AI-based metadata vetting + human review queue, not a fake simulated ID3/ACR/fingerprint animation (the design's own `startScan()` was pure `setTimeout` theater with no real check behind it; porting that faithfully would have shipped a fake sense of security on a copyright-sensitive surface, so this reuses real infrastructure instead of translating the mockup literally). **(3) SEO/robots policy — decided, not deferred**: formalized keeping the current indexed behavior (see row 94's updated entry) rather than reverting; reversing is the harder-to-undo action (deindexing costs real search ranking even if reverted again), current state shows no evidence of harm, and there's no clear business-owner directive either way — a one-line `robots.ts` change if the original privacy intent still matters. **(4) Prisma 6→7 and TypeScript 6→7 — declined, not attempted**: checked both changelogs before touching anything. Prisma 7 requires ESM module format, a new generator/output config, driver adapters, a new `prisma.config.ts`, and removes client middleware — and this exact codebase already had a real production outage from a subtle Prisma config regression on Cloudflare Workers (row 177), with a dedicated test now guarding the exact config this upgrade would touch. TypeScript 7 is Microsoft's in-preview native Go compiler ("Project Corsa") — explicitly incomplete (no declaration emit, no `--build` mode, unfinished editor tooling), not production-ready. Both are real tech debt, deliberately not rushed without a dedicated migration/test cycle. tsc clean, vitest 260/260 (34 files, 6 new), lint clean (612 files), guard:design clean, production build succeeds. **Not runtime-verified against a live DB** (same sandbox limitation as every schema-touching row before this one) — the migration SQL needs to actually run against production before `pinnedStats`/the vetting extension take effect; recommend a smoke test on a real profile once deployed. | PATCH /api/profile-editor (pinnedStats field), POST /api/artist-media (vetting extended to all uploads, no new endpoint) | ✅ Done (code) |

Root cause was two compounding bugs, both present on Artist and Venue (and a milder form on the DJ/Promoters and Fan pages): **(1)** `FollowButton` and `ShareButton` are shared components dropped into each profile's hero-actions row alongside page-local `Link` buttons (`.artist-hero-btn` etc.) — but `FollowButton` styled itself entirely inline (7px padding, 11px font, pill radius 7) and `ShareButton` defaulted to the unrelated global `.button.small.secondary` class, so the row mixed three incompatible button systems with different heights/radii/fonts. **(2)** The mobile media query forced every child to `flex: 1; min-width: 0` with no `white-space: nowrap`, splitting the row into equal-width slots regardless of content — "+ Follow" (the only multi-word label) was the first to overflow its slot and wrap internally. Fixed by giving `FollowButton` an opt-in `variant="hero"` prop (matches the exact padding/radius/font-size every `*-hero-btn` class already uses; the other 6 call sites — RadioHome, PagesHome ×2, Discover, and the two default-branch fan/DJ usages — keep the original compact chip style, unchanged) and passing an explicit `className` to `ShareButton` on Artist/Fans so it matches too. Replaced the equal-flex mobile rule on Artist/Venue with the same non-wrapping horizontal-scroll pattern their own `.artist-tabs`/`.venue-tabs` already use correctly (`flex-shrink: 0; white-space: nowrap` + `overflow-x: auto`) — matches the original `Artist.dc.html`/`Venue.dc.html` design intent (`flex-wrap:nowrap; overflow-x:auto`), which the live translation had drifted from. DJ/Fan hero rows don't have the forced equal-width rule (they wrap to a second line instead of squeezing text), so the `variant="hero"` fix alone resolves them; left their mobile CSS untouched. tsc clean, vitest 254/254, lint clean, guard:design clean, production build succeeds. **Not screenshot-verified against a live render** — this sandbox has no DB connection to render real profile data; the fix is a straightforward CSS/prop change reasoned through against the exact markup and stylesheet, recommend a quick mobile-viewport check once deployed. | — (UI/CSS only, no API change) | ✅ Done (code) |
| 205 | src/middleware.ts, next.config.mjs, scripts/lint-source.mjs, src/lib/auth.config.ts, src/lib/csp-nonce.ts, CLAUDE.md | **Sitewide issue audit (2026-07-13, requested: "Audit site for issues") — plus a CI-caught revert of this row's own first attempt.** Full health sweep: `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vitest run` 254/254, `guard:design` clean, source-policy `lint` clean, `npm audit --omit=dev` 0 vulnerabilities (the 17 moderate dev-only `@opentelemetry` advisories under Sentry/lighthouse are the same pre-assessed, no-fix-available ones from row 191/192 — not new), production build succeeds. Checked all 6 `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` call sites — all safe (static content, the existing `toSafeJsonLdString()` sanitizer, or an opaque Prisma-generated ID, never raw user text). **Tried and reverted:** the production build emitted `"middleware" file convention is deprecated. Please use 'proxy' instead"`. First attempt renamed `src/middleware.ts` → `src/proxy.ts` per the official codemod (mechanical: file + default export name, `config`/matcher unchanged) — locally, `tsc`/`vitest`/`lint`/`guard:design`/`next build` all stayed green, so it looked safe and was pushed as PR #505. **CI's workerd-smoke job caught what local checks couldn't:** `ERROR Node.js middleware is not currently supported. Consider switching to Edge Middleware.` Root cause (confirmed via Next.js's own proxy.js docs): `middleware.ts` always defaulted to the Edge runtime; Next.js 16's `proxy.ts` convention **defaults to the Node.js runtime instead**, and critically "the `runtime` config option is not available in Proxy files — setting it will throw an error," so there's no way to opt back into Edge from a `proxy.ts` file. This project deploys to Cloudflare Workers via `@opennextjs/cloudflare`, which does not support Node.js-runtime middleware — hence the CI failure. **Reverted the rename in full** (file, function name, the `scripts/lint-source.mjs` CSP/nonce check that reads it by path, and all comment references in `next.config.mjs`/`auth.config.ts`/`csp-nonce.ts`) and added an explicit **"do not rename to proxy.ts"** note with the reason to `CLAUDE.md`'s critical-backend-wiring table, so a future session doesn't reattempt this and hit the same CI break — the deprecation warning is a known, accepted cost until OpenNext/Cloudflare adds Node.js-runtime middleware support. **Also spot-checked and ruled out as issues:** several undocumented-in-CLAUDE.md routes (`/this-weekend`, `/for-you`, `/journal`, `/community`, `/status`, `/launch`, etc.) are all real, populated pages, not dead stubs — just missing from the page-map table (a documentation gap, not a functional one); an a11y grep for icon-only buttons flagged `MobileQuickGrid.tsx`'s search button, but it renders a visible `{searchPlaceholder}` label alongside the icon — false positive, not an issue. | — (no net route/API change; middleware.ts unchanged from before this row) | ✅ Done (code) — audit clean, attempted fix reverted after CI caught a real incompatibility |

---

## Quick Commands for Claude Code

```bash
# Pull latest design files from this project
# (Claude Design exports; Claude Code imports)

# 1. Check what changed
cat DESIGN_SYNC.md | grep "⏳ Pending"

# 2. View the design page
open https://claude.ai/projects/[project-id]/[page].dc.html

# 3. Implement + commit
git add src/app/[route]/page.tsx
git commit -m "sync: implement [page] from design — DESIGN_SYNC #[n]"
git push origin main

# 4. Verify live
open https://ihype.org/[route]

# 5. Mark done in DESIGN_SYNC.md
# Change ⏳ Pending → ✅ [commit SHA]
```
